beginning a minor Mercury <term>☿</term>, and not that of the seven nights—which has been killed seven times and brought back to life—then one must first overcome this unripe Mercury <term>☿</term> by means of an effective Salt <term>🜔</term> <note>The symbol 🜔 usually denotes Salt or a mineral solvent</note>. The overcomer must be stronger than that which is to be overcome, and herein lie many delays; for while some can produce the Tincture <note>a concentrated substance capable of transmuting base metals into gold</note> in a single month, others must toil for many months and endure much danger. There are so many various types, ways, and Tinctures to be made here that one can almost not count them. For one can precipitate the philosophical Mercury <term>☿</term> by means of all metallic and mineral Salts <term>🜔</term> which are of an effective nature, and turn it into a fixed, tinting precipitate <term>Præcipitat</term>. One then resolves this precipitate again in its own tinting oils and poisons <note>original: "Giffſten" — likely referring to corrosive acids or potent mineral reagents</note>, makes it fixed once more, and repeats this as often as one wishes. Through this, the Tinctures are heightened more and more, and are able to color far more parts than before. No man has yet described this as clearly as I am doing now; whoever understands and finds it will learn to recognize the gratitude he owes me.

### LXXIV. Roger Bacon, an Englishman, has written: "If you return the equal weight of the elements, you will see pleasant gifts with your eyes"; what does he mean to indicate by this?

**Answer.** The equal weight of the elements is understood in many ways. He does not mean that one should weigh out equal parts on a scale, but rather he means the weight of nature: the **active** <note>original: "agens"</note> and the **passive** <note>original: "patiens"</note>, until these have balanced each other, [so that] all elements no longer struggle against one another. For who can weigh the air and fire? But when the elements are united and have their equal weight, one sees the pleasant gifts, which are the red and white **Tinctures**. However, one does not require the weight until the **Composition**, and specifically first when Water and Earth, as husband and wife, are to be joined together; thus the wife must be manifold. For first she must congeal with the Earth, and also [become] as Earth

become earth; for then the water must soften the entire earth, dissolve it, and make it thin, so that it becomes water. This water must rot <note>putrefy</note>, becoming black and stinking, turning into black earth; this black earth afterwards becomes the permanent earth, and is washed white by the **Azoth** <term>Azoth: the universal solvent or "mercury of the philosophers," considered the essential spirit of any substance</term>, its own water, and transformed into the **foliated earth** <term>original: "Terram foliatam"; a purified, receptive state of matter often compared to fertile soil</term>.

For the second time, the weight comes into play again when so many **Eagles** <term>Eagles: alchemical symbols for cycles of sublimation or the volatile spirits that lift the "fixed" matter</term> are brought forth, which are meant to tear apart the **Lion** <note>original: "♌" (Leo); represents the fixed, solid principle, often gold or antimony</note>. Here, the most fortunate number should be from 7 to 10; and this does not mean 7 or 10 times the weight of the Eagle and one part of the Lion, but rather it signifies the **imbibition** <term>original: "Eintranckung"; the process of a solid "drinking" or absorbing a liquid until saturated</term> or **sublimation** <term>Sublimation: the process of heating a substance to a vapor and cooling it back to a solid to purify it</term> of the Eagles.

If one counts all the first small imbibitions of short duration, there will be 10; but generally, one counts only the last ones, which are the true imbibitions, consisting of the sevenfold rotation of the wheel, through which the Eagles are prepared 7 times—that is, sublimated—in the sieve of nature. Also, it is a matter of weight when I dissolve this star <note>original: "*"; likely referring to the "star-regulus," a crystalline form of antimony used as a starting material</term> or Eagle in its air or oil, which are unequal parts.

When, through these unequal and adverse things, such magnificent fruits are revealed only by the **active** <term>original: "Agens"</term> and the **passive** <term>original: "Patiens"</term> principles, and these are all brought together into one so that they no longer struggle with each other, but the strife has ceased—then they are left to rest together, the active and passive reconcile, and no **disturbance** <term>original: "Turba"; a state of chaotic conflict or "turmoil" between the elements</term> remains, nor any further acting or suffering. Then the glory of the world is won, and the equal weight is returned to the elements.

<summary>This page describes the Nigredo (blackening) and Albedo (whitening) stages of the Great Work, detailing how "Eagles" (volatile spirits) must break down the "Lion" (fixed matter). It concludes by explaining that the "equal weight" of the elements is achieved when the active and passive principles cease their internal struggle and reach a state of rest.</summary>
<keywords>Nigredo, Albedo, Azoth, foliated earth, Eagles, Leo, imbibition, sublimation, active and passive, Turba, elements</keywords>

LXXV. Melchior of Lebanon <note>Melchior Libinensis: likely referring to the 16th-century Hungarian alchemist Melchior Cibinensis, famous for describing the alchemical process in the form of a Mass</note>, a Hungarian, has written:
Just as in the beginning the tender
child is nourished by snow-white milk;
so must this stone be fed with pure
milk; what does he mean
by this?

Antw. He means by this the first composition, when it is nourished by the **Virgin’s Milk** <term>Virgin's Milk: original: "Jungfer-Milch"; a white, milky liquid or solvent used to dissolve or "feed" the substance in its early stages</term>, so that it grows and increases, and becomes larger; and **Mercury** <note>original: ☿</note> is the naked child, which must be fed until it reaches its manly years. Then it is fed by the air, and is the **Chameleon** <note>In alchemy, the chameleon represents the stage where many colors appear—the "peacock's tail"—as the matter begins to react to heat</note>, which takes on all colors and lives on the air; finally, it is fed by the fire, and is the **Salamander** <note>A mythical reptile believed to live in fire; it represents the final stage of the work where the matter is stable and "fixed" even in extreme heat</note>, which lives in the fire.

LXXVI. Michael Sendivogius, a Pole <note>Michael Sendivogius (1566–1636): a world-famous Polish alchemist credited with discovering oxygen long before the modern era, referred to here as "the Pole"</note>, has written:
**Saturn** <note>original: ♄; represents lead or the "heavy," cold principle</note> moistens the earth, which,
O **Sun** and **Moon**, possesses your flowers;
what does he want with this?

Antw. He wants to make known that one should pour and moisten the **foliated earth** <term>original: "geblätterte Erde"; a purified state of matter ready to receive the spirit</term>, which contains the flowers of the philosophical Gold and Silver, with the **Oil of Saturn** <note>original: ♄-Oehl</note>; through this, the volatile, fiery, living **Sun** <note>original: ☉</note> and **Moon** <note>original: ☽</note>—which is the **Solar Mercury** <note>original: ☿ solaris</note>, the white volatile Sun—may become fixed into white **Sulfur**, which Saturn must wash with his **urine** <note>original: "Harn"; alchemical code for a salty or acidic mineral solvent, often used for purification</note>, that is, moisten it; and this oil has no use in the entire art,

**LXXVII.** What do they mean by the animals, such as the **Ram**, **Lion** <note>original: ♌</note>, **Ox**, **Bear**, **Wolf**, **Eagle**, **Phoenix**, **Peacock**, **Raven** and **Swan**, **gilded Adder**, **Dragon**, **Serpent** and **Toad**?

**Antw.** By the **Ram**, they understand **Mars** <note>original: ♂</note>, because it is his house in the heavens; by the **Lion** <note>original: ♌</note> the **red earth**, within which is the **philosophical Sun**, for the Lion is the Sun's house—named by many, known by few. The **Ox** or **Bull** is the house of **Venus** <note>original: ♀</note>, by which the **Menstruum** <term>Menstruum: a solvent or "mother-liquid" that dissolves a solid substance; here linked to the feminine, receptive nature of Venus</term> is indicated, which is of the nature of **Venus** and the stars, which blow fire and smoke through the nose; the Bull has the sign of the **Moon** <note>original: ☽</note> upon its head, which shows that this **Venus** must be turned into the **Moon**; **Venus** allows herself to be suppressed by her, and leaves the governance to the **Moon**. 

The **Bear** signifies the old **Saturn** <note>original: ♄</note>, whose oil is of a **Venusian** kind because he has played so much with **Venus**; this precipitates **Mercury** <note>original: ☿</note> and turns it into a white, fixed **Sulfur** <note>original: 🜍</note>; this is the copulation of **Mercury** and **Venus**. 

The **Wolf** is the great hunger in this **Saturnine** animal, wherefore he eats the King all at once. The **Eagle** is **Mercury** <note>original: ☿</note>, as is the **Phoenix** when it is colored yellow by its own oil, the **gilded Adder**; this **Phoenix** burns itself and a more beautiful one arises from the ashes. The **Peacock** signifies the variegated colors <note>often called the "Peacock's Tail," a stage where many iridescent colors appear in the flask</note>; the **Raven** the blackness <note>Nigredo, the stage of decay and darkness</note>; the **Swan** the whiteness <note>Albedo, the stage of purification</note>. When it is moistened by the arsenical oil so that it is yellow, it is the **poisonous Dragon**. When it is still raw and poisonous, it is the **Serpent** or **Stone-Serpent**...

LXXVIII. I would also like to have several figures, emblems, texts, and verses of **M. Daniel Stolcius von Stolzenberg** <note>Daniel Stolcius von Stolzenberg (1600–1660) was a Bohemian physician and writer, famous for his alchemical emblem book 'Viridarium Chymicum' (The Chemical Pleasure Garden)</note> interpreted and explained, such as:

# The First Figure

->Emblem, text, and verse, containing four <term>operations</term> <note>or chemical procedures</note>, namely: <term>solution</term> <note>original: Solutio</note>, <term>cleansing</term> <note>original: Ablutio</note>, <term>conjunction</term> <note>original: Conjunctio</note>, and <term>fixation</term> <note>original: Fixio</note>.<-

The text and verse are: Behold, here four sisters are painted by the same lot; these recount what your work looks like. The first commands that the body, having been set aside, should be dissolved. The second, how the material is to be washed with fine skill. The third gives you the means to bring together again the parts that were divided from one another. The fourth teaches you to fix the Stone in the fire.

# Interpretation

The four sisters are the four <term>operations</term> in the subsequent work. The first commands the dissolution of the body that was set aside, to make the double **Mercury** <note>original: ☿</note> through water and earth; this is the first **composition**, when the woman dissolves the man, and the **Mercury** <note>original: ☿</note> dissolves <note>original: solvirt</note> its earth or fixed part, whereby the **blackness** <note>often called 'Nigredo,' the initial stage of decay and transformation in the alchemical process</note> appears. The second commands this blackness to be washed away by the **Virgin’s Milk** <term>Virgin's Milk: a metaphorical term for a purifying solvent, white mercurial water, or a specific stage of distillation</term>. The third teaches the second **composition**—to make the **Stone**—and to unite the separated parts.

# The 2nd Figure or Emblem.
## The Philosophers' Stone from Spirit, Soul, and Body.

**Text and Verse:** Just as here the serpent has three necks, and yet only one body, in which it holds a cruel poison hidden: So also does the <term>propagation</term> <note>The process of "multiplying" or increasing the power of the alchemical substance</note> have three natures, of the Sun and of the Moon, and has a cruel poison in its body; therefore also the raven, peacock, swan, and king provide the colors, of which you, when you see them with your eyes, can be assured.

## Interpretation.

The serpent is the unique <term>material</term> <note>original: Materia</note>, which initially in its raw state contains the three <term>principles</term> <note>original: Principia</note>—Salt <note>original: 🜔</note>, Sulphur <note>original: 🜍</note>, and Mercury <note>original: ☿</note>—within itself and is poisonous; but during the work, when these three are separated from it, purified, and put together again in the <term>propagation</term> and <term>generation</term> <note>The stages where the substance "reproduces" its qualities</note> where Sun and Moon are shown, it becomes even more poisonous, and first [turns] black, thereafter variegated with many colors, and finally white—the "King in his shirt" <note>A traditional alchemical metaphor for the Albedo or white stage of the work, signifying purity</note>—and the Stone in Soul, Spirit, and Body. When one sees these, one is assured that one can have the white Sulphur <note>original: 🜍</note>, the white <term>tincture</term>.

## The 4th Figure or Emblem.
### *Calcination* <note>The process of heating a substance to high temperatures to drive off volatile elements, reducing it to a powder</note>, the 1st Degree of the Philosophers.

**Text and Verse:** Here at the table sits the Cyllenian <note>Cyllenius, a name for Mercury/Hermes, born on Mount Kyllini</note> with outspread wings, whose two sides are enclosed by the Sun and the Moon. Upon the table grow the herbs and flowers placed there, and the Lion <note>original symbol: ♌︎</note> devours the serpent, the Mercury <note>original symbol: ☿</note>. See, the light spirit is fixed <note>original: figiret; rendered stable or solid</note> by the lime <note>The powdery residue left after calcination</note>; therefore, the spirit rejoices when it is united with the earth through its own sprouting and the greening of the flowers.

## Interpretation.

The Cyllenius <note>Cyllenius is an epithet for the god Mercury/Hermes</note>, whose two sides are enclosed by the Sun and Moon, is our living water, which consists of soul, spirit, and body; within it, the Sun and Moon have their powers dissolved, and in this water, the most magnificent herbs—the tinctures—grow. The Lion <term>♌︎</term> devours the serpent, Mercury <term>☿</term>, turning it into calx <note>A fine powder or "lime" produced by heating a metal</note>, and calcines it, because one must rely not on what has been sublimated, but on what has been calcined. Now this calx must be moistened with the growing Menstruum <note>original: Menstruo; a term for a powerful alchemical solvent</note> and must sprout and turn green with its own earth, so that one nature may rejoice in the other, and this light spirit is fixed <note>made stable or solid</note> by the calx; thus, our greening and growing ore becomes our Sun <term>☉</term> <note>Gold or the perfected state</note>.

# The 5th Figure or Emblem.
### Another Degree, Solution <note>original: Solutio; the process of dissolving a solid substance into a liquid</note>.

Now dissolve the Mastery <note>original: Magisterium; the medicinal or transformative substance being worked upon</note>, which has been made ready by the aforementioned calx, so that Mercury <term>☿</term> may rise up through pleasant signs.

## Interpretation.

The Lion <term>♌︎</term> heated by the air or heaven is the Solar Mercury <note>original: ☿ Solaris</note>, which is drawn out by the air or the heavenly Menstruum <note>solvent</note>, which the heaven

## The 6th Figure or Emblem.
### The third Degree, Separation <note>original: Separatio</note>.

**Text and Verse:** The dissolved bonds shall fall away; that which is light shall rise on high, while that which is heavy shall sink down, so that each may be in its own proper place. From this comes the heaviness of the earth, which will cast down the lightness from the heights and, through a contrary degree, humble the proud hearts <note>This imagery describes the volatile "spirits" being fixed or stabilized by the solid "earth" of the matter</note>. Only the lightness of the air, mixed with the dissolved heaviness, will be able to adorn the same through its rays.

## Interpretation.

In all solutions <note>the process of dissolving solids into liquid</note>, separations <note>original: Separationes</note> occur; however, philosophical separations are not violent like common ones, which devour everything—pure and impure—without distinction. But in this solution, both separation and conjunction <note>joining together</note> occur, for the light spirits come down again and bring the body and soul with them...

# The 7th Figure.

## The 4th Degree, <term>Conjunction <note>The union of the previously separated elements, often symbolized as a marriage</note></term>.

Just as the longed-for sun rises after mournful clouds, so too does a greater love rise after anger. The bodies which you have separated, now bring them together again, so that the seed may gladden you with many children. Meanwhile, Neptune <note>In alchemy, Neptune represents the watery solvent or the "sea" of the matter</note> can prepare a lukewarm bath so that the man and woman can bathe.

### Interpretation.

When the sun has endured its darkness, after the mournful, murky clouds—for here no such blackness is to be seen in the <term>multiplication <note>The process of increasing the quantity or quality of the alchemical medicine</note></term>, or rather in the composition—it instead becomes only somewhat dark in color, very slightly blackish, dark, sky-blue, violet-blue, and reddish, until bright colors appear again, and it <term>multiplies <note>increases in potency</note></term> in its power. Should it be further <term>multiplied</term> and elevated, the baths must be heated and prepared anew.

## The 10th Figure.
### Seventh Degree, <term>Cibation <note>original: Cibatio; from the Latin "cibus" (food), this stage involves "feeding" the material to increase its substance and power.</note></term>.

Here our child is fed by mother’s milk, so that it may increase and receive new strengths. The triple-threefold measurement brings forth the rigid dragon, which bears the fruit of the Sun and the Moon. On one side, the triangle shows three eagles, and these you can hand over to the three spirits.

## Interpretation.

The child, thus fed by mother’s milk, is our coagulated Mercury, or has been soaked by the <term>Virgin’s Milk <note>original: Jungfer-Milch; a symbolic name for a specific white solvent or nourishing liquid used to hydrate the dry "stone."</note></term>, whereby it is finally transformed into the hard, rigid dragon, which has the fruit of the Sun and the Moon in its body. On one side he has three eagles, the <term>three Principles <note>original: 3. Principia; refers to the three alchemical foundations: Salt, Sulfur, and Mercury.</note></term>, which is his birth-line from which he consists; these must be handed over to the three spirits. The first spirit is the <term>Oil of Saturn <note>original symbol: Oleum ♄; a preparation derived from lead, used here for its stabilizing qualities.</note></term>, through which it becomes fixed; the other spirit is the <term>vegetable menstruum <note>original: vegetabilische Menstruum; a solvent or "solvent of life" derived from organic matter.</note></term>, in Soul, Spirit, and Body, which dissolves this fixed <term>precipitate <note>original: Præcipitat</note></term>; the third 

<summary>This page describes the transition from the solidification of the matter (Congelation) to its nourishment (Cibation). The "child" or alchemical product is fed with "Virgin's Milk" to transform it into a powerful, rigid dragon representing the union of solar and lunar qualities.</summary>

The third spirit is the <term>red man <note>original: rothe Mann; a symbol for the active, solar principle or the final stage of the work.</note></term>, the fire, through which it becomes the <term>red tincture <note>original: rothe Tinctur; the elixir or powder used for transformation.</note></term>; when this fire is boiled in this water, the <term>short path <note>original: kurze Weg; refers to the 'via brevis,' a more direct but more intense alchemical method.</note></term> is shown here.

## The 11th Figure.
### Eighth Degree, <term>Sublimation <note>original: Sublimatio; the process of vaporizing a solid and condensing it again to purify it.</note></term>.

So that our body may be turned into thin air, it is raised once more from the low place into the height. The King brings back the <term>phoenix <note>A mythical bird representing rebirth and the transition to the red (rubedo) stage.</note></term>, the Queen the <term>swan <note>A symbol for the white, purified stage of the work (albedo).</note></term>; and then the <term>wolf <note>Often a symbol for antimony or a corrosive agent used to "devour" impurities in metals.</note></term> emerges from his caves. The growing fruits stand upon the <term>Apollonian tree <note>The solar tree, representing the growth of philosophical gold.</note></term>; therefore Time mows down the ripe grass with his scythe.

### Interpretation.

Our sublimated body is the white swan, which has floated upon the <term>philosophical sea <note>The liquid medium or solvent used in the vessel.</note></term> and has become dry white <term>sulfur <note>original symbol: 🜍</note></term> through the fire of <term>calcination <note>The process of heating the matter to a high temperature to reduce it to a powder.</note></term>. It was burnt by the <term>oil of Saturn <note>original: Oleum ♄; typically refers to a lead-based liquid or the dark stage of decay.</note></term>, which is the hungry, devouring wolf that consumes this body and returns it improved. It is now the "roasted swan," the food of the King, because the white sulfur has thereby become of a hotter, drier, and more solar nature. From this, the phoenix is brought forth, who must burn himself through fire. And the fruits grow upon the Apollonian tree—upon the fixed, precipitated <term>mercury <note>original symbol: ☿</note></term>, which is called the "raging Apollo," for his rays kill the mercury, which he transforms into true white <term>gold <note>original symbol: ☉; in this context, the "white sun" refers to the perfection of the white stage of the Stone.</note></term>. The hay-harvest begins, where the green grass—the white tincture, which appears somewhat greenish—...

## The 12th Figure.

### The Ninth Degree, <term>Fermentation <note>original: Fermentatio; the process of adding a 'leaven' to the alchemical matter to stabilize it and allow it to multiply in power.</note></term>.

The seed which was given to the cultivated earth comes forth through the soul, after it has been <term>recalled <note>original: revocirt; meaning the spirit or soul is reunited with the purified body.</note></term>. Now our trumpet calls, and the bodies that were entrusted to the grave shall rise again and be adorned with new bodies. For without ferment, the Sun does not rise into the heights, and the beautiful <term>Diana <note>The Roman goddess of the moon, here representing the purified white stage or silver.</note></term> does not renew her life once more.

## Interpretation.

The newly risen bodies rise step-by-step through <term>exaltation <note>original: Exaltation; the process by which a substance is raised to a higher state of purity and power.</note></term> into the heights, through their own ferments and coloring <term>poisons <note>original: Giffte; in alchemy, 'poisons' often refer to the potent, raw energies of the minerals that must be transformed into medicine.</note></term>, which cannot kill these risen bodies; for they die within these bodies and become <term>tinctures <note>The potent liquids or powders capable of transforming other substances.</note></term>. And there are two ferments: one of the Sun, the other of the Moon. These are not common <term>gold <note>original symbol: ☉</note></term> and <term>silver <note>original symbol: ☽</note></term>; likewise, those substances that are to be fermented are not common gold and silver, but rather our boiled-out, fixed <term>sulfur <note>original symbol: 🜍</note></term>, both white and red. This is far nobler than common gold and silver and has different parents. Common gold and silver cannot be a ferment; they have heavy, fixed, compact bodies. How then could they raise this red and white sulfur, making it ferment like a dough, and

Once he has filled this body, it is high time to cast <term>golden clamps <note>A metaphor for stabilizing or "fixing" the volatile spirit so it does not evaporate.</note></term> upon it, through which it is preserved. For it thereby receives a passive object in which it can work as an active force, which it destroys and melts down, and is sustained again through this <term>fixed <note>Stable and resistant to heat.</note></term> body, as this spirit assumes a body in which it can rest. Otherwise, it would be irretrievably lost and would have to vanish back into its <term>chaos <note>The state of primeval, disorganized matter.</note></term>.

# The 14th Figure.
## The Eleventh Step: Multiplication <note>original: *Multiplicatio*. This is the stage where the power of the Philosopher's Stone is increased, allowing a small amount to transform a large quantity of base metal.</note>.

The shining Queen is carried upon a magnanimous **Lion** <note>original symbol: ♌</note>, and she holds, O Pelican, your young. But the **Lion** <note>original symbol: ♌</note> nourishes through his own flesh countless many young who play with their father. Likewise, he can also bring forth many young of the **Stone**, and can multiply them without end.

## Interpretation.

The shining Queen is the white **Tincture**; the green **Lion** <note>original symbol: ♌. The "Green Lion" often represents a strong solvent or the raw, vital force of nature used to dissolve the "body" of the metal.</note>—through him, she is transformed into the glassy sea <note>The "glassy sea" (referencing Revelation) represents a state of perfect purity and transparency in the alchemical work.</note>, which must now be united with the fire of the red **Lion** <note>original symbol: ♌</note>. Through this, it multiplies itself into many parts, and in such a manner can she be multiplied.

# The 16th Figure.
## Seven Metals.

Here you see the inner secrets of the earthly world revealed, and the high stars upon the mountainous places. Namely, the earth itself receives its own planets, to which the elements offer their powers. If you doubt which they are, only look at the metals with watchful senses; thus the peak of heaven or the heights will become known to you.

# Interpretation.

Our pure metallic earth or salt receives our planets through the <term>saturation</term> <note>original: *Eintranckung*. A lineage or sequence of transformations.</note> descends. Associated here with the metal tin.</note> appears, which already yields <term>meteors</term> <note>original: *Meteora*. In this context, it refers to atmospheric-like changes within the alchemical flask, such as "winds," "clouds," and shifting colors.</note>—wind, clouds, and colors in the material—for the body is not yet volatile, but rests in the throne of <term>Jupiter</term> <note>♃</note>. ## Figure 17.
## The State of Marriage.

In our heaven, two lights shine at once, which bring back the great lights of the heavens. Join these two together, just as a wife is given to a husband, so that a lawful marriage-bed may come of it; for this reason, the transformation of the elements must also become <term>metrical</term> <note>original: *metrisch*. In this context, it refers to reaching a state of perfect proportion or balance.</note>, so that the round form may bring forth its powers.

## Interpretation.

The two lights of heaven are our Sun and Moon; the Moon is our fixed white <term>Sulfur</term> <note>Symbol: 🜍. The "Sulfur of the Wise" is not common brimstone, but a philosophical principle of heat and action.</note>, and the Sun is our red <term>Sulfur</term>. These two are married as husband and wife, so that the elements are inverted: the man into the woman, and the woman into the man—the <term>Mercury</term> <note>Symbol: ☿.</note> [into] <term>Sulfur</term>, and the <term>Sulfur</term> [into] <term>Mercury</term>. Fire cooked in water transforms the entire nature of the water into fire. This could not happen sooner; the water first had to be dried out and turned into Earth, and the Earth into Air; only then could the fire burn in the air and water, because it could no longer be extinguished by the moisture of the water.

In the <term>Mercury of the Wise</term> <note>Symbol: ☿. This refers to "Philosophical Mercury," a refined spiritual substance rather than the common liquid metal found in thermometers.</note> is what the Sages seek; namely, in the Mercury of the bodies. These are not the seven common metals, but are derived from its own bodies, all of which originate from the body of <term>Magnesia</term> <note>In alchemy, "Magnesia" refers to the "First Matter" or the raw, chaotic material used at the start of the process, rather than the modern mineral magnesium.</note>. For our substance, in its external purification, appears in the form of a clear water, like spring water; but when such a serpent dissolves its own red earth, and this water becomes slimy and white, thick and heavy, it is called the <term>Pontic water</term> <note>A name for a potent alchemical solvent, often described as a "sea" that dissolves the philosophical gold.</note>. 

When this [water] dissolves its own central earth or fixed body—the little <term>Green Lion</term> <note>Symbol: ♌. The "Green Lion" is a famous alchemical symbol usually representing a strong acid or the raw, "unripe" state of the matter that has the power to consume or dissolve metals.</note>—and has ascended on high with it as with its own fixed part, then it is truly the Mercury, the Body. In this is what the Wise seek, for this <term>foliated</term> <note>original: *geblätterte*. This describes a flaky or layered crystalline appearance of the matter during the chemical process.</note> volatile earth does not fear its own fire, which comes from its father. It is its innate <term>Sulfur</term> <note>Symbol: 🜍. The active, masculine, and combustible principle in alchemy.</note>; they have lain as brother and sister in one body. 

And although it flees when this fire touches it, this fire and this flame flee along with it, because they both cannot escape one another. Thus, one nature preserves the other, and they delight the worker through their child—the <term>Tincture</term> <note>The "Tincture" is the Philosopher's Stone in its liquid or powder form, capable of "tinging" or transforming base metals into gold.</note>—which they have begotten, and through which the worker becomes blessed.

<summary>This page explains the nature of the "Mercury of the Wise," describing how the union of volatile and fixed principles (the Green Lion and the Pontic Water) produces the final Tincture. It emphasizes that the true alchemical Mercury is a "Mercury of the bodies" derived from a primordial Magnesia.</summary>

<keywords>Mercury of the Wise, Magnesia, Pontic Water, Green Lion, Sulfur, Tincture, dissolution, Philosophical Mercury</keywords>

## The 19th Figure.
->Tincture.<-

The cruel beast pursues the delicate maiden through pathless fields, and grinds with a terrifying sound; Mercury <note>Symbol: ☿</note> cries out: "Hurry here, you beautiful nymph, with swift feet, and do not fear the cruel danger of your grave. Whoever dies with me shall also, after mournful misfortune, joyfully rise again to the desired riches."

## Interpretation.

The cruel beast is the **Dragon**, which is also called the **Bath of Hell** <note>In alchemy, the "Bath of Hell" refers to a powerful acidic solvent or a stage of intense heat/dissolution that "destroys" the initial form of the matter.</note>. The delicate maiden is the **foliated earth** <note>original: "geblätterte Erde," also known as *Terra Foliata*. This refers to the purified solid matter that has reached a flaky or crystalline state.</note>; because this is of a double nature, both masculine and feminine, it is sometimes taken for the man and sometimes for the woman. The Mercury who cries out after her is the **Menstruum** <note>A "Menstruum" in alchemy is a universal solvent capable of dissolving solids into their primary essence.</note>, which is prepared from Mercury and consists of Soul, Spirit, and Body, which is our **living water**. In this very water, the **white fixed Sulfur** <note>Symbol: 🜍. Sulfur represents the active, masculine principle and the "internal fire" of the substance.</note> must sink, melt away, and be born anew therein, multiplying itself and rising toward the riches of wealth. Here, the **white tincture** is shown: how to shape, augment, and multiply the **White Stone** of the first order, into which no fire enters, but rather the wise spirits.

## The 20th Figure.
->The Water of the Wise.<-

Sometimes the woman mixes the various...

## Interpretation.

The **Water of the Wise** is but one water: the **Nile**, which springs from **Paradise** and pours itself into four streams, the **elements**. From these spring the waters of health and wealth; the elemental aspect is the abiding water with the earth; it also blackens and whitens the earth. From these springs the **Tree of the Living Water**, just as the water of health and wealth flows from the **Sanctuary**; the other waters are the coloring poisons; when it is said, "dye the leaf with poison," it is <term>venenum tingens <note>original: "venenum tingens," Latin for "dyeing venom" or "staining poison." In alchemy, "poison" often refers to a potent, transformative substance that "kills" the old form to create a new one.</note></term>. But with the fiery water, this linen is dyed red; both of these are permanent colors. The first white color is the abiding water and our white, dried-out earth or starch <note>original: "Stärcke," likely referring to the solid, fixed residue or essence of the matter.</note>. Upon this, when it is spread out thinly and **sublimated** <note>the process of heating a substance so it turns to vapor and then cools back into a solid, used for purification.</note> into a thin sheet—becoming a leaf <note>original: "folio."</note>—we then strengthen this leaf, which is hungry and thirsty; we cool it with the **oil**, by which it becomes soft, mild, and supple; we soak the colors in using the oil so that they remain permanent and cannot be washed off again,

...through the **fire-bath** it is fully refined, purified, and dyed blood-red; the white **tinder** <note>original: "Zündel." ## The 21st Figure.
### Twofold Sulfur.

See, here come two lions with necks joined together, entering into a strong bond of friendship with one another. You, who seek the **Ferment** <note>a substance used to "ripen" or transform the base material, similar to how yeast transforms dough.</note>, join the two **Sulfurs** <note>represented by the symbol 🜍 in the original text.</note> together so that you may multiply your load. Let one be fixed and the other rise into the air, but when they are united, they must stand and remain in a single, harmonious degree.

## Interpretation.

The two lions here are the two **Sulfurs**, the Red and the White, which must be brought together into one **Sulfur**. This cannot happen until it is dry and **fixed** <note>stable and resistant to change when heated.</note>; otherwise, the Red has no **ingress** <note>original: "Ingreß," meaning the ability to penetrate or enter into the other substance.</note> into it and will always float on top. However, through the correct means—the <term>medium of joining <note>original Latin: "medium conjungendi." In alchemy, this is a third substance or specific process that allows two opposing elements to bond.</note></term>—they pass into each other. The **Fixed** holds the **Volatile**, and they become one.

## The 22. Figure.

->Elixir.<-

Whoever wishes to recognize the Elixir through the birds with an eager mind, so that the metals may be made fit for your work, you must first seek such a **medicine** <note>original: "Medicament." In alchemy, this refers to the Stone or Elixir in its capacity to "heal" or transform base metals into gold.</note> which flows before the swift flight of **Mercury** <term>☿: the volatile, liquid principle of matter</term>. Finally, this Queen must be reconciled with the **Sulfur** <term>🜍: the active, fiery, and masculine principle</term> of the King; thus, the bird flying away will be deceived <note>here meaning "trapped" or "fixed"</note> by the [other] bird.

### Interpretation.

Here, the depiction of the **White Sulfur** <note>symbolized by 🜍</note> is described. Because it is the volatile **Eagle** <note>the eagle often represents the process of sublimation—where a solid turns to gas—or the "volatile" substance that rises in the flask.</note>, the other bird—which is elsewhere called the flying **Dragon’s Head** <term>☊: traditionally the North Node of the moon; in this context, a specific chemical reagent</term> and also the flying **Saturn** <term>♄: usually representing lead, but here described as "flying" or volatile</term>—must be added to it. Also, the **Red Man** [must be placed] into the **White Woman**, our volatile **Sun** <term>☉: Gold or the masculine spirit</term> and **Moon** <term>☽: Silver or the feminine soul</term>.

When these two are rightly united according to the weight of nature, the natural treasure—the **White Elixir**—is produced from them. This is somewhat hard to melt and is called the **Water-Stone of the Sages** <note>a paradoxical name suggesting the Stone has the qualities of both a solid and a liquid.</note>. However, where the weight <note>the specific proportions or ratios of the ingredients</note> is not achieved, only one of these birds rises on high and escapes; no seal helps then, because the **Hermetic Seal** <note>an airtight closing of the glass vessel, often using heat or special clay</note> was not correctly applied. Either the vessel shatters with a bang, or the **White Sulfur** (the **Mercury**) smokes out and is not held by its adversaries. But whoever achieves this has won everything, and these two will rise on high and return again to the bottom, until they both

...turned into a black pitch; this lasts for a good while, and the artist cannot help this second **blackness** as he could with the first, which he was able to wash away from the black **Latona** <term>Latona: a mythological reference to the "dross" or dark matter of the Work; in myth, Latona was the mother of Apollo and Diana (the Sun and Moon)</term> with water. ## The 23. Figure.
->The Union.<- <note>original: "Conjunctio."</note>

> Shut the caught birds in two glasses, the mouth-openings of which you must join well together; one will flee from it, the other will hold back the course and will no longer ascend into the heights. A strong solvent or "spirit" used in the process.</note>, through which the White Tincture is multiplied into infinity <note>original: "in infinitum."</note>. By this, the Tincture is made fusible and flowing when it is joined with its oil.

## The 24. Figure.
->Augmentation.<- <note>original: "Augmentatio."</note>

Here you have three glasses to see, likewise the strong jaws of the Lion [♌] <term>♌: the "Lion" represents the fixed, sulfuric principle or a strong corrosive acid</term>, also the Mercury [☿] <term>☿: the volatile, metallic principle</term>, which the swift wing makes light. Take three parts of the medicine, put them together with the flying [volatile] one, and he will soon lose his wings in the hot fire. From this will be our Lion [♌], which you can multiply again in the furnace with the same care according to your pleasure.

### Interpretation.

The three glasses are the crystal of Mercury [☿], or the glass-like Azoth <term>Azoth: the universal solvent or essential transformative agent; the "A" and "Z" represent the beginning and end of the work</term> of Lull <note>referring to Raymond Lull, a major figure in medieval alchemy</note>, which becomes the frozen sea; for the Mercury [☿] must be devoured by the strong jaws of the Lion [♌], by the Green Lion [♌] and by the Red Lion [♌]. The first transforms it into the White Tincture; the other makes it red into the Red Tincture, the red glass. These three stones are all for medicine and can be increased. The swift wings of Mercury [☿] are the two increasing and multiplying solvents <term>Menstrua: alchemical liquids used to dissolve or "feed" the matter</term>, both of the White and the Red Tincture. Here also, the weight is indicated to some extent: the fixed part must be three or four times heavier than the volatile part, otherwise it could not coagulate <note>original: "c-aguliren," the process of turning a liquid into a solid</note> it and make it fixed.

Four sisters divide the treasure quite skillfully among themselves, and each takes her share of it: The first can heal all human illnesses; the second takes away the sick members of the metals <note>referring to the "healing" or transmutation of base metals into gold</note>; the third transforms common stones into precious gemstones; and the fourth makes

## Interpretation.
<note>original: "Auslegung"</note>

The four sisters are the four general virtues of the Stone; however, the Stone of Mercury <term>☿: the volatile principle</term> is already medicine enough, the white tincture even more so, and the red tincture above both of these; these three stones are the "Universal" stones, and they must come together into one single stone. For the crystalline Stone of Mercury is transformed into the white tincture, and that into the red, both of which latter ones "tinge" <note>to "tinge" or "tincture" means to transform the inner nature and color of a base metal into a noble one</note> the metals, transforming them into Gold <term>☉: Sol or Gold</term> and Silver <term>☽: Luna or Silver</term>. But before the white tincture becomes entirely red, crystals and other lesser stones can be transformed into precious gemstones through it; however, when it is very red and flowing, it also makes glass ductile and flexible if a significant quantity of it is melted upon the glass. Likewise, the "oils" produce their own spirits which, when transformed into fixed glasses, become fluid and flexible if they are frequently imbibed <term>imbibed: the process of repeatedly adding liquid to a dry substance until it is saturated</term> with the oils.

## The 27th Figure.

->Water of Life.<-
<note>original Latin: "Aqua - vitæ"</note>

Here living water springs from living wells, which can give you much benefit for your life. Here the resolved stars <term>resolved stars: celestial influences or planetary metals that have been chemically "opened" or dissolved</term> expand their powers; therefore the Moon and Sun wash their faces. For this reason, hurry quickly here and quench your thirst by the hot Sun.

## Interpretation.
<note>original: "Auslegung"</note>

Here the two multiplying menstruums <term>menstruum: a solvent used in alchemy to dissolve or extract the essence of a substance</term> are described: one belonging to the Sun <term>☉: Gold</term>, the other to the Moon <term>☽: Silver</term>. These are both our industrious Sun and Moon—the Red Sun and the Blessed Water <note>original Latin: "Aqua benedicta"</note> that does not wet the hand, the mineral bath, and the philosophical Royal Water <note>original Latin: "Aqua Regis"; traditionally a mixture of acids that dissolves gold, here used for a spiritualized solvent</note> which gives birth to the metals anew. Within this, the dissolved stars <note>original: "Gestirne," referring to the planetary influences or "souls" of the metals</note> expand their powers; these are our white and red sulfur <term>🜍: the active, masculine principle in alchemy</term>. When they are dissolved therein, they attain higher and higher tinctures. Common gold and silver can also be washed and renovated within it, and brought to the state of tinctures, though they do not "tinge" <note>transform other substances</note> as much as the Great Work of Nature. He who cannot find the former will find his thirst quenched here. He who cannot obtain the heavenly Nectar to drink must be content with wine; it strengthens him nonetheless.

## The 28th Figure.

### The Philosophical Ore.

Our greening ore is the philosophical Sun, for it purifies our bodies through its rays. If, however, you do not know how to join this with our Duenech <term>Duenech: an alchemical code name, often referring to Antimony or the "Green Lion" that devours the sun</term>, it will not be a desired marriage-bed of the wedded state. But when two lions—two strong hearts—come together, the golden juice shall flow forth from their mouths.

## Interpretation.

The philosophical Sun is threefold, for our—

<meta>This page continues the technical explanation of the alchemical process, building on the previous discussion of the "greening ore" and the mysterious substance known as Duenech. It moves from the materials themselves to the nature of the fire and the baths required to perfect the work.</meta>

<page-num>82</page-num>

<header>* ):( o ):( *</header>

Our three heavens each have their own Sun. First, the Astral [heaven], in which our stars rise; this is the "little golden book," which, together with the Duenech <term>Duenech: an alchemical code-name, often referring to antimony or the mineral 'body' that acts as a solvent</term> in the second composition of the greening ore, has become the emerald. This is the "visible gold" <note>original Latin: "Aurum perspectibile"</note>, where the Sun is still hidden behind the Moon, until at last it rises in a deep sanguine red. The third [heaven] occurs when the two lions are joined together—the green and the red ♌ <note>the lion symbol represents both a mineral solvent and the fixed state of the matter</note>—which is the red tincture.

## The 29th Figure.
### The Philosophical Fire.

This fire is pleasant, warm, and simultaneously moist; it preserves everything and lets nothing perish. Namely, it is uniform and well-suited to generate things, tempering each thing with its own balance. From this fire, the Sun and his own wife <note>the Moon, or the feminine principle of silver/mercury</note> by no means flee; instead, he enters into it with her charmingly, as if into pleasant baths.

## Interpretation.

The philosophical fire does not burn the hands, but rather melts down the bodies; it purifies them in soul, spirit, and body, and renovates them. Our fire makes tinctures out of our Sun <note>gold or the masculine principle</note> and Moon <note>silver or the feminine principle</note>. When these are washed anew and bathed therein, they are thereby elevated once more, in both quantity and quality.

## The 30th Figure.
### The Philosophical Bath.

You must have a water of such a kind that not a single limb becomes wet; in this water, you must now arrange for the Sun to bathe with the Moon. When you have put this into effect, the light spirit will unite with them, and you will see with your two lilies; thereafter, every tree will bring forth its own fruits, and then you can pluck the apples.

## Interpretation.

The philosophical bath is the *menstruum* <term>menstruum: a term used by alchemists to describe a solvent or "mother-liquid" that can dissolve solid matter into its primary components</term> in soul, spirit, and body; it is the water that does not make the hand wet. It dissolves the husband and wife, Sun and Moon <note>original: "Sol und Luna," referring to gold and silver or the masculine and feminine principles</note>. When these two have bathed therein, the poisonous water <note>often a reference to "mercurial water" which is corrosive yet transformative</note> will unite with them, and the two lilies will be visible—the red and white tincture, or rather the red and white fire <term>△: the alchemical symbol for fire</term>. Then one can pluck the apples from the Garden of the Hesperides <note>In Greek mythology, these were golden apples of immortality; in alchemy, they represent the successful completion of the Great Work</note> and enjoy their fruits.

## The 31st Figure.

->Conjunction<- <note>original Latin: "Conjunctio," referring to the union of substances</note>

Here Gabricus lies joined together with his beloved Beja <note>Gabricus and Beja are traditional alchemical personifications of the fixed/solar and volatile/lunar principles, often depicted as a brother and sister or king and queen</note>, but she encloses the lover within her womb. Thus, those who were once two grow together into one body, so that they may give you the pleasant gifts of their delightful marriage bed. Just as the hen has need of a rooster, so also does the Moon need the Sun, through which it <note>the Moon</note> desires to multiply its generation.

<summary>This page explains the "philosophical bath," a solvent that dissolves the solar and lunar principles to produce red and white tinctures. It illustrates this through the "Conjunction," the symbolic sexual union and merging of the figures Gabricus and Beja.</summary>

## Interpretation.

I have already explained who Gabricus and Beja are; they represent Water and Earth, Mercury <note>original symbol: ☿</note> and Salt <note>original symbol: ⊖; while this symbol often represents Earth, the text here explicitly identifies it with Salt as the "fixed part"</note>. The Salt is the fixed part that coagulates the Mercury. This simple Mercury rejects all unions except for that with its own spouse; from that spouse alone does it accept intimacy. Therefore it is said: This serpent has bitten itself. These two are the dragons, one of which has wings while the other has no wings <note>In alchemical art, the winged dragon represents the volatile (spirit/gas) and the wingless dragon represents the fixed (solid/body)</note>, which guard the Golden Fleece and the apples in the Hesperian Garden. This Beja, the Virgin Mercury, receives the Salt into her body until she makes her husband volatile and turns him also into Mercury; then he is called the "Mercury of the Bodies." If they are then to increase, a new marriage must take place so that they may multiply their kind.

## The 33rd Figure.

->Extraction of the Soul or Impregnation,<- <note>original Latin: "Animæ extractio sive Imprægnatio"</note>

The bodies, which had rotted in a sealed grave, now receive once again the shining gifts of a new soul. They are now united with these gifts and are simultaneously retained by them; thus, one is eased through the help of the other. Only have patience, and be quick with your hand as well as with your keen contemplation, so that the spirit does not fly away from the warm hearth <note>The "hearth" refers to the Athanor or alchemical furnace, where temperature must be carefully controlled to prevent the volatile spirit from escaping the vessel</note>.

## Interpretation.

The bodies are our Sun and Moon when they lie in putrefaction <note>the "black" stage of alchemy, called *nigredo*, where the material breaks down</note>; the soul and spirit

# The 34th Figure.
## Washing or Purification. <note>original Latin: Ablutio vel Mundificatio</note>

A completely quiet rain falls down from the heavens and washes away our bodies with its drops. Therefore, that black color disappears into the thin air, and immediately the limbs possess a lovely whiteness. For what reasons do these become so pure? Because the pure spirit is not yet harmonized with them, for they are already pure without it.

## Interpretation.

When the bodies have lain for their appointed time in the grave, and the spirit begins to circulate, it rises into the heights and acts as an intermediary <note>original: schwischent; likely referring to the spirit becoming a vapor between the liquid and the top of the vessel</note>. Then a rain falls down; the drops wash away the blackness. When the little white streams of the Moon <note>original: Luna</note> come, then the pure spirit unites with the body, and together they become a dry spirit.

# The 35th Figure.
->The Soul’s Rejoicing, or Sublimation.<- <note>original Latin: Animæ Jubilatio, sive Sublimatio</note>

Now at last it is time to rise from the marriage bed and to leave the mournful grave; now sounds the sweet, lovely rejoicing of the souls. Namely, the soul is accustomed to being gladdened when the body offers both obedience and service to it. In this way, one bird rescues the other—which is submerged in the clear, bright waters—from the worry of drowning and submergence.

### Interpretation

When the spirit goes back into the body, it makes both body and soul living, refreshes and gladdens them, and these two become obedient to the spirit. They allow themselves to be led upward by it and follow the spirit, ascending and descending with it. Thus the fleeting bird <note>original: flüchtige Vogel; a term for a "volatile" substance that evaporates easily</note>, which was submerged in bright waters, takes the other bird with it into the heights, where the water-bird <note>the "fixed" or solid substance</note> dries itself in the air and can submerge with it again, until they become a single bird.

It carries three snakes and a bird in its hands, and nearby is a tree adorned with branches and magnificent fruits, from which you might now count out countless riches—provided, however, that you [do] not [lack] the right root <note>The text cuts off here, continuing with the catchword "right" on the next page.</note>

## Interpretation.

The two-headed body is Water and Earth, Mercury <note>the symbol ☿ in the text refers to Mercury or Quicksilver</note> and Salt, beneath which are the lunar horns <note>crescent moons</note>, which represent the "white Mercury." In its hands are three snakes; this Mercury already consists of Soul, Spirit, and Body. Even if no visible spirit appears to be incorporated with it, nevertheless both Water and Earth have spirit within them. For the simple elements do not act on their own: if the Earth had no <term>spiritum</term> <note>Latin: "spirit" or "breath of life"</note> or spirit, it would be dead; and if the Water did not have a spirit from the air within it, nothing could grow from it.

However, when the Water unlocks the Earth (acting as the Salt), the spirit that was bound within the Salt is freed from its bonds. It then grasps the spirit of the Water as if it were its "little wife," and they unite with one another. Thus, the "double body" comes forth, in which the third part—the spirit—is also present. For it is a living body that has received new life out of "dying" and "death" <note>referring to the previous alchemical stage of Putrefaction, where matter "dies" to be reborn</note>. 

The bird that it has with it is the life-giving <term>menstruum</term> <note>from the Latin *menstruus*; in alchemy, this refers to a powerful solvent capable of dissolving and transforming metals</note>, which is volatile and consists of Soul, Spirit, and Body. Within this solvent, the three snakes (which are the snake with three necks) must be <term>resolved</term> <note>dissolved or broken down into their fundamental parts</note>. From this, another new body will grow forth again, in a far better form than before. Through frequently repeated labor, an untold number of fruits can be harvested. But whoever [the roots...]

## Die 37. Figur.

->Fermentation.<-

Combine the true ferment with the unique Sulphur <note>the symbol 🜍 stands for Sulphur, the active, fiery principle</note>, so that you can easily display the birds. These two you must then joyfully cast into our sea; from there, the living flame will exert its powers. Thus you can give the seed to the "foliated earth" <note>also called "terra foliata"; a purified state of the matter that appears flaky or layered like leaves</note>, which will be quite fittingly prepared for the warm hearth.

## Interpretation.

Fermentation occurs several times in the <term>philosophical work</term> <note>the process of creating the Philosophers' Stone</note>. First, the ferment of the Water is the Earth, and the Earth is the yeast <note>literally "lees" or "dregs"</note> of the Water, through which both become a dough and ferment. After that, there is a fermentation of the "Red Man" and the "White Woman" <note>symbolic names for the solar/masculine and lunar/feminine principles, often Gold and Silver</note>. It is also called a fermentation when the Mercury is fixed <note>rendered solid or non-volatile by heat</note>, where one volatile bird must hold the other bird, just as when the proper claws are attached to this falcon. 

The true fermentation occurs when the white, fixed Sulphur is dissolved in its white oil, and the bird is immersed in the clear, bright waters—this being the "water that does not wet the hands" <note>an alchemical term for a theoretical universal solvent that dissolves metals but does not act like common water</note>. The other ferment is the red oil of the Sun’s <note>Gold's</note> Sulphur, the fire of the Stone. Here, however, it is shown how the Gold-

## The 40th Figure.

### Fixation. <note>original: "Fixatio"; the process of making a volatile substance stable and resistant to fire</note>

Here in this grave lies our venerable body; by it stands the spirit, but the mind, reason, and sense return to heaven; therefore, ensure that this returns again to heaven, and from there flies down again to the very bottom! In this way, it will unite with the pleasant forces and, through its help, make the body alive again.

## Interpretation.

This is the representation of the white Jupiter <term>♃; here representing a stage of purification or the metal tin</term>, and now truly grown in soul, spirit, and body into a perfect man. Through the many ascents and descents <note>the process of distillation and condensation</note>, it was first separated into soul, spirit, and body, but afterwards reunited; for when these vapors rise up, soul, spirit, and body seize one another, and because no watery moisture is present here anymore, but everything is glutinous <note>original: "glutinosisch"; sticky or glue-like, indicating the stage where the substance begins to cohere</note>, one seizes the other during the ascent and fixes <note>original: "figirt"; renders stable and non-volatile</note> it, so that it immediately falls to the ground as a fixed substance. ## The 41st Figure.

## Interpretation.

Here the washing of the black earth is taught, which is here called the mother, because the earth is the mother of all things. This must now, as mother and child, be washed white and purified from its blackness. This also signifies the serpent Hydra <note>the many-headed water serpent of Greek mythology</note>: whenever one head was cut off, more always grew in its place. ## The 42nd Figure.
->Revivification.<- <note>original: "Revificatio"; the alchemical stage of bringing a "dead" or fixed substance back to life or volatility</note>

Here stands the shining Moon with her bright brother <note>the Sun</note> rising again from the pure well with a fresh face; meanwhile, the Pelican splashes its young—its most beloved children—with blood from its own breast. From this, they afterwards receive new strength in their wings and hearts, and the life-giving power warms their tender limbs.

### Interpretation

This Revivification is understood to mean that the fixed Mercury <term>☿; the spirituous principle of the Great Work, here in its stabilized form</term> must be made volatile <note>turned back into a vaporous or active state</note> once again through the restoring solvent <term>Menstruum; a specialized alchemical liquid used to dissolve or transform solids</term>, which consists of spirit, soul, and body, and is made from the very Mercury of the philosophers <term>☿. philosophorum</term>. This must call the Mercury back so that it may be unlocked and enter into the air. Here the saying goes: give Mercury to Mercury, and the fixed Mercury will be turned into oil by this active Mercury, uniting itself with these heavenly powers, whereby it also becomes heavenly.

## The 43rd Figure.
->Perfection.<- <note>original: "Perfectio"; the completion of the alchemical process where the Stone reaches its highest power</note>

See, the King carries a strong chain in his left hand, which he has wound around the neck of the Lion <term>♌; the symbol for the fixed, solar principle or purified gold</term>. In his right hand, he holds a rod and points to the monstrous serpent. Nearby is the proud tree with your fruits, O Sun; if you wish to pluck them, first seek the root. There shall be no fruit except that which comes from its own seed.

### Interpretation.

The King here is the Red Tincture <note>the final, perfected state of the alchemical medicine</note> or the red fixed Sulfur <term>🜍; the active, masculine principle of the Work</term>; he is the strong chain that he has wound around the neck of the Lion <term>♌; the symbol for the fixed or solid material</term>. When he is led away from this Lion or dissolved, he holds such a lion fast to himself and is, as it were, firmly bound to him with a chain. Through this, he becomes the perfect Red Tincture. The rod with which he points to the snake is the natural mountain-rod <note>original: "Berg-Ruthe"; a divining rod used by miners to find ore</note>, from which the King himself had his beginning; and now everything has become a single snake, from which the ripe golden fruits are to be plucked. But whoever wishes to pluck them must first seek the root of this Sun-Tree, which is the Mercury of the philosophers <term>☿. philosophorum; the "living" spirit of the metal</term> made from the snake. This is the tree that bears the Sun <term>☉; gold</term> and Moon <term>☽; silver</term> fruits.

## The 44. Figure.

->The Green Lion.<- <note>original: "♌. Viridis"; a symbol for the acidic solvent or the raw, "unripe" matter of the Great Work</note>

What does the starry Lion signify? What of the image of the shining Sun which he possesses? Tell me that, dear Muse! That is our hero, the Green Lion, in the innermost entrails of whose belly the red stars lie hidden; therefore he gives off those lazy Apollonian colors <note>original: "Apollinische Farben"; colors associated with Apollo, the sun god, often appearing during the heating process</note> and is the downward-striking brilliance in the sea.

### Interpretation.

The Green Lion is our Sun transformed into the White Tincture <note>the penultimate stage of the work, capable of transmuting metals into silver</note>. The woman who is pregnant by the Sun and has the Moon under her feet will now soon become the rising Sun. It is

## The 45. Figure.

->Our Gold.<- <note>original: "Aurum nostrum"</note>

Here Apollo sits upon the throne in royal garments, and beside him sits the beautiful Moon with flowing hair; the Son is in the middle, whom they crown together at once, so that he may be ready to rule their blessed kingdoms. What does this signify? Without the Sun and his spouse, this Little One of ours will be unfit for the kingdom.

## Interpretation.

The Apollo in royal garments is our red Sulfur <term>🜍; the active, masculine principle of the Work</term>, the fixed matter of the Stone, the King in the golden piece; the Moon with flowing hair is the White Tincture <note>the silver-stage of the alchemical medicine</note>. These two marry anew and beget the Sun-Son, whom they both also crown together. For one alone cannot generate, and without these two, the third would not exist.

## The 46th Figure.

->The Resurrection of the King.<- <note>original: "Resuscitatio Regis"</note>

Now the highest power has been given to our King, and he carries such great gifts from the paternal present. Although he overcomes everything, he is nonetheless overcome by a servant: this servant dissolves the King's limbs through his sprouting forth; however, the Mother gathers the dissolved limbs, and so both the Mother and the servant receive great honor from it.

## Interpretation.

Our multiplied red Tincture <note>the final medicine or "Stone" in its red, solar form</note>, as the Son of the Sun, now possesses the greatest power in the metallic kingdom; he can place golden crowns upon all. If he wishes to multiply himself further, however, he must allow himself to be killed by a servant. Because no one is his equal, his former oils and spirits are very lowly compared to his current highness and nobility; yet, since they sprang from his previous blood—from his grandparents, who were only poor peasants and subjects—they dissolve his limbs. But because the King possesses such great virtue and is in his previous bath of blood and sweat, the lowly blood is improved by the Royal blood. The fixed Jupiter <term>♃; tin, representing a stable, metallic state</term> maintains the volatile one, and the volatile one dissolves [the King]; and once again, through this struggle, the King becomes high and glorious, expanding his power and dominion. His land—the earth—is increased, and he tinges <note>original: "tingiret"; the alchemical act of transforming the nature and color of a base metal into gold</note> even much further than before.

## The 47. Figure.
### Three Fountains of Everlasting Water. <note>original Latin: "Tres Fontes perennis Aquae"</note>

We three watery fountains are seen within a cavern of a hidden heavenly rock, and are adorned with heavenly waters. To us, the Sun <term>☉; representing the solar, masculine principle or gold</term> and Moon <term>☽; representing the lunar, feminine principle or silver</term> give their powers, and all the stars are favorable to us through their rays. Whoever drinks from us shall live according to a fittingly renewed youth, and continue his desired whiteness <note>the "Albedo" or whitening stage of the alchemical process, signifying purification</note>.

## Interpretation.

The three watery fountains in the hidden caverns of the heavenly rock are: the first is the white water, the Virgin’s Milk; the second is the Blood; and the third is the Oil. All three are produced by the hard rock. These three fountains can increase the weights of the philosophical gold and silver. Also, the Sun and Moon will grow from this and yield their fruits within it once they have become the waters of health. Whoever drinks of it will live in constant youth—that is, in constant health.

## The 48. Figure.
### The Nymph of our Sea.

I, who was once conceived and born of a fluid father who so often flows around the entire earth, I pour out for you from our breasts milk with blood, which two give cooked, pleasant weights of gold.

### Interpretation.

The Nymph is our Moon <term>☽; representing Silver or the feminine principle</term> <note>original: Luna</note>, begotten from the simple, flowing Mercury <term>☿</term> <note>original: Mercury</note> of the wise; she gives milk and blood to the white and red Mercury <term>☿</term>, which are the pleasant weights of gold, when red and white are united together and both are colored red.

## The 49. Figure.
### Our Mercury <term>☿</term>.

Arrange it so that the Eagle <note>The "Eagle" in alchemy typically represents the volatile principle or the process of sublimation</note> is united with the Lion <term>♌; representing the fixed principle, often Gold or Antimony</term> <note>original: Leo</note> through burning love, and, being weakened, sheds its tears; thereafter, unite the tears with the Lion’s blood, and you shall joyfully possess the riches of the whole earth. For when they are united together through the fire after the manner of the salamander <note>A mythical lizard believed to live in and endure fire, symbolizing the "fixation" of a substance so it no longer evaporates</note>, they shall despise the powers of the lashing and wanton flames.

### Interpretation.

Here it is described how to fix our volatile white Mercury <term>☿</term>, where the Eagle and Lion <term>♌</term> must hold the head; as soon as the Lion tears the Eagle apart, there are drops and vapors. These fall upon the earth, upon the Eagle and Lion, until one swallows the other and is transformed into a white, dry tincture <note>A "tincture" is a potent alchemical substance capable of transforming or "coloring" base metals into noble ones</note>. These dry tears are brought through the fire into the red tincture by the red Lion <term>♌</term>; thus it is the salamander, which lives in the fire.

# The 50th Figure.
## The Philosophical Egg.

Here our Dragon is present, who rests through the sprouting of the Sun, and whom anyone can obtain for a small amount of money <note>original: "gering Gold"—likely a scribe's error for "Geld" (money), a common alchemical trope asserting that the "First Matter" of the Stone is cheap and found everywhere</note>. That which he lies upon also rests within him at the same time; therefore your Egg is called the deep Turba <term>Turba; referring to the "Turba Philosophorum" or the "commotion," a state of chaotic agitation of the elements during the alchemical process</term>. But what do so many shining crowns mean? He is preparing golden gifts for his brothers.

### Interpretation.

Our Dragon is the Mercury <term>☿; the "Mercury of the Wise," the fundamental solvent</term> of the wise, which keeps the Sun <term>☉; Gold or the masculine principle</term> and Moon <term>☽; Silver or the feminine principle</term> swallowed within itself. For it is itself the Sun and Moon that it has swallowed, and it transforms this devourer into another form. That which it lies upon also rests within it: the Egg, the red and the white <note>The "red" and "white" refer to the two stages of the Great Work: the Albedo (white) and Rubedo (red)</note>, in which union the Turba is to be seen. This Dragon costs little money, and when it has consumed all its own poison, it can transform metallic bodies into Gold <term>☉</term>.

# The 51st Figure.
->Generation.<- <note>original Latin: "Generatio"</note>

See, there an Old Man rests in a hideous cave within a rock, to whom a Raven <note>The Raven symbolizes the "Nigredo" or black stage of decay and putrefaction necessary for rebirth</note> has come through a bond; his spirit and sense have left his limbs, so that he may be redeemed and freed from the punishment of his evil deed. As they now come together and are reunited with that very same spirit, our Apollo <note>In alchemy, Apollo represents the perfected Solar state or the Golden Tincture</note> is born from these three.

<vocab>philosophical Egg, Dragon, Turba, Mercury, Sun, Moon, Generation, Raven, Apollo</vocab>

<summary>This page describes two alchemical emblems: the "Philosophical Egg" containing the Dragon (Mercury) that swallows the Sun and Moon, and the "Generation" stage where an Old Man (Lead/Saturn) and a Raven (Putrefaction) give birth to a new solar principle (Apollo).</summary>
<keywords>Philosophical Egg, Dragon, Mercury, Sun, Moon, Turba, Generation, Raven, Apollo, Nigredo, Alchemical symbols</keywords>

<header>❀ ):( ( 0 ) ):( ❀</header>

### Interpretation.

The Old Man is our Saturn <term>♄; Saturn, the planetary symbol for lead and the initial, "heavy" state of matter</term>, who must be separated into Soul, Spirit, and Body; for the spirit and soul must leave the body, otherwise it cannot die. The spirit and soul may rest within it for a time, but not permanently <note>original: "nicht mit Bestande"—meaning without stability or lasting endurance</note>. But when the body putrefies, and is illuminated and quickened again with the spirit and soul after its glorified resurrection, then it is Apollo <note>Apollo, the sun god, represents the perfected Gold or the successful completion of the Great Work</note>, our Royal Body, which is no longer subject to any corruption; instead, they dwell within it from then on with permanence. This is the new birth.

## The 52nd Figure.
->Conjunction.<- <note>original Latin: "Conjunctio"—the chemical wedding or union of opposites</note>

Sun and Moon carry the most beautiful lilies in their hands, and in the center of that place, our Apollo is seen; wherefore they both unite their own powers, and hence he bears the royal scepter with a strong hand. His radiance is praised throughout the whole world, and his honor and praise flow into the wandering throng <note>original: "herumschweiffende Rotte"—this may refer to the lesser metals or the uninitiated who have not yet reached this state of perfection</note>.

### Interpretation.

The beautiful flowers or lilies, which Sun and Moon carry, are the two <term>triangle</term> <note>Alchemical symbol for Sulphur</note>—the red and the white. From these, the Tincture <term>Tincture; the transformative essence of the Philosopher's Stone</term> grows greatly when they unite their powers. From this union, the great and powerful King emerges afterward, who rules in the entire metallic and mineral kingdom: the Son of the Sun.

# The 53rd Figure.
->Death<- <note>original Latin: "Mortificatio"—the alchemical stage where the matter is "killed" or stripped of its initial form to be transformed</note>

Now, honor grows envious of our shining King, and ten youths from the common rabble kill him. Everything becomes confused; the Sun and the Moon give many signs of their sadness following the darkness that has arisen. A rainbow, painted with many colors, appears, bringing to the people the welcome news of peace.

## Interpretation.

The King who is killed is the red Sulfur <term>Sulfur; representing the active, masculine, and "fiery" principle of the alchemical work</term>. This is the Tincture <note>The "Tincture" is the potent medicinal or transformative essence being created</note> which is dissolved once more in its original waters. Within these waters, the Sun and Moon rot when they are eclipsed in the Dragon’s Head <note>original: "Drachen-Haupt"—this refers to the ascending node of the moon, symbolizing a time of transition or "swallowing" of light</note>. Finally, the sign of grace—the rainbow of many colors—appears when the strife has settled and the heaven of Mercury <term>Mercury; representing the spirit, fluidity, and the "volatile" principle that must be stabilized</term> has run its course. Thus the Tincture is multiplied; the ten youths are ten parts of the Menstruum <term>Menstruum; a powerful solvent or "mother water" used to dissolve the solid matter</term>.

## Interpretation.

The city, so stormed and devastated by enemies and fire, is the rich city built by all the gods, wherein our King has his residence. The common rabble is under the influence of Saturn (♄) <note>Saturn represents the heavy, leaden, and dark stage of the process</note>, whose red spirits fix <term>Fix; the process of making a volatile substance stable and resistant to fire</term> Mercury (☿). They kill the Royal Body; yet when this body decays and is once again united with the strong, double spirits, this King shall rise again to a new life in an improved form.

## The 55. Figure.
->Whiteness<- <note>original Latin: "Albificatio"—the stage where the blackened matter is washed and purified until it shines white</note>

Behold, a sister who has returned to life goes before her beloved brother, bearing white limbs in a crystalline manner. She accuses the brothers that, having despised the heavenly gift, they have loved nothing but the burdens of the earth; she admonishes those who are like her brother that they might be restored to the stars, and their head given a new crown and ornament.

## Interpretation.

Here is first described the White Tincture, which precedes the Red Tincture. This is prepared through the Green Lion (♌) <term>Green Lion; a name for the raw, acidic mineral or solvent—often vitriol or antimony—used to dissolve gold</term>, when such is well rectified <term>Rectified; purified through repeated distillation</term> and the green Duenech <term>Duenech; an alchemical term for a specific mineral spirit or the "green" state of the matter</term> is separated from its fire (⏃) in the highest purity. It looks like diamonds when it has been multiplied; but if it is the Stone of the first order—the natural treasure—it looks somewhat green. For when the pure Mercurial (☿) heaven unlocks its sweet water, Venus (♀), acting as the Green Lion (♌) who played into Saturn (♄)

## The 56. Figure.

Here is the union of all opposing things, because from the watery Azoth has now become the red and dry Azoth,

### The 57th Figure.
### The Dream or the Vision.

A radiant man is there, during the quiet night-time, and his most secret limbs appear to my eyes. His limbs are covered with a green garment, and his head is adorned with a beautiful crown; above him is a bright star that illuminates the thick darkness, and as I wonder who he might be, he commands me to follow him.

### Interpretation.

To configure the philosophical <term>Mercury (☿)</term> <note>The "philosophical Mercury" is not common quicksilver, but the purified "spirit" or "water" of the metals used in the Work</note> and to make the composition of the Stone is not always the same path, even if the same materials are taken; for many philosophers have made it in 7 months, some in one month. Here, precisely the shortest path is not described, but rather how the Mercury (☿) should be configured with the green <term>Leo (♌)</term> <note>The "Green Lion" usually refers to Vitriol or raw Antimony ore, considered a ravenous "devourer" of metals in the early stages of the process</note>, when the Mercury (☿) is precipitated with the property of <term>Venus (♀)</term> <note>Venus/Copper; often associated with the color green in alchemical reactions</note> and appears greenish. But when he is called back again through his own Mercury (☿) and revived in the <term>Water of Life</term> <note>original: "Aqua-vit"—the "living" solvent or spirit used to re-animate the "dead" matter</note>, he becomes bright and white; for the Mercury (☿) must follow him and enlighten him, turning the green into white. When a body is to be transformed, it must happen through a spirit, and he who overcomes <note>The text breaks here to transition to the next page; "overcomes" likely refers to the spirit overcoming the physical resistance of the body</note>
