### CXIV. What then is the preparation of the Eagle’s Embers? <note>original: *Adlers Gluten*; "Eagle" in alchemy often refers to the volatile spirit or sublimated substances, while *Gluten* refers to the heat or the "glow" of the process.</note>

**Answer:** It is the making of our slimy water, once it has been coagulated into a crystal.

### CXV. What then is Antimony or the Lead of the Philosophers? <note>original: *Antimonium oder Plumbum Philosophorum*</note>

**Answer:** It is the red <term>Dragon's Head: A symbol (☊) often representing the North Node in astrology, but in this context, it refers to a specific refined state of Antimony.</term>, and the *Lili* <note>A Paracelsian term referring to the "lily" or the purest essence of a substance.</note> of the Ancients; the <term>Antimony: Represented by the symbol ♁ (the earth with a cross).</term> is twofold: one is the common black kind, and the other is the "female," which is white and is called leaden marcasite <note>original: *marcasitta plumbea*.</note>.

### CXV. How have they described the work that is to be undertaken with it? <note>The source repeats the numbering CXV.</note>

**Answer:** They have described it thus: one should dissolve it in distilled vinegar; however, this is not the vinegar that women make behind the stove, but is called **philosophical vinegar**. <note>original: *philosophischer Essig*; a specialized alchemical solvent, often a highly rectified acid rather than common household vinegar.</note>

### CXVI. How does one make the blood of the <term>Dragon's Head</term>? <note>original symbol: ☊</note>

**Answer:** One must pour one’s own philosophical vinegar onto the red calcined <term>Antimony</term> <note>original symbol: ♁</note>, and it will turn a deep red. This is then gently distilled over, and the blessed oil of this mineral comes forth in blood-red droplets like actual blood; the treasures of the entire world are not to be compared to it.

### CXVII. How then does one dissolve silver in the philosophical manner?

**Answer:** It is dissolved in 6 parts of our "Calcining-Water," also called <term>Virgin's Milk: A traditional alchemical name (Lac Virginis) for a white, milky-looking solvent or precipitate.</term> <note>original: *Jungfer-Milch*</note>, and gently distilled off until it appears as an oil. Afterward, the green water is dissolved and distilled off again; in three rounds, the <term>Silver: Represented by the symbol ☽ (the moon).</term> is turned into oil, whereupon our...

If the dissolving spirit of wine <note>original: *Spiritus vini solvensis*</note> is greater, it takes into itself the <term>Sulfur: Symbol 🜍; representing the combustible, active, or "soul" principle of a substance.</term> of the <term>Moon: Symbol ☽; representing Silver.</term>, which one can separate and reunite.

### CXVIII. How then does one dissolve the <term>Sun: Symbol ☉; representing Gold.</term>?

**Answer:** One dissolves it in the red Virgin-Milk <note>original: *rothen Jungfer-Milk*; an alchemical metaphor for a specific solvent, often a distilled acid or alkaline solution that appears white or cloudy before turning red.</note>, and cohobates <note>To redistill a liquid repeatedly over its own solid residue to concentrate its properties or extract an essence.</note> it until it is turned into oil, or also separates the <term>Sulfur</term> from it using the dissolving wine, and joins them again; or one dissolves them both through our Water of Life <note>original: *Lebens-Aqua-vit*</note>, which consists of Spirit, Soul, and Body.

### CXIX. What then is the Burning Water, or Water of Life?

**Answer:** It is neither brandy nor spirit of wine, but our Water of Life <note>original: *Aqua-Vitæ*</note> that makes [things] living, which is the Spirit of <term>Mercury: Symbol ☿.</term> and has its beginning from <term>Saturn: Symbol ♄; typically representing lead or the "prima materia."</term>.

### CXX. What then is the philosophical fire? Is it of one kind, or is it understood in many ways?

**Answer:** First is the glowing white fire, which is also called Mary's Bath <note>original: *Balneum Mariæ*; a water bath used to provide gentle, consistent heat.</note>, as well as our horse-dung <note>Decomposing horse manure was used by early chemists as a natural, low-temperature source of steady heat for fermentation or digestion.</note> and secret furnace, which must be applied to its own vessel; this vessel is called the green vessel <note>original: *vas viride*</note> of <term>Saturn</term>. In this fire, Pontanus <note>Referring to Joannes Pontanus, a famous alchemist who wrote about the "secret fire" that works without the aid of human hands.</note> erred more than two hundred times, even though he worked with the correct matter. 2. We have the fire "against nature," which burns more fiercely than the fire of hell: the Stygian Water <note>original: *Aqua Stygia*; usually a reference to a powerful acid like aqua regia that dissolves metals.</note>. 3. We have a fire which is a yellow <term>Sulfur</term> oil. 4. We have a fire, our Solar-Fire, the glowing red [fire].

### CXXI. What is the <term>Antimony: Symbol ♁; in this context, it refers to the "Philosophical Antimony," a specific starting material or "First Matter," rather than the common mineral.</term> of Nature?
**Answer:** It is our white <term>Antimony: Symbol ♁.</term>.

### CXXII. What do you do with it?
**Answer:** It must be dissolved into oil, so that it becomes the incombustible oil <note>Incombustible oil is a symbolic term for a substance that has been "fixed" or stabilized by fire so that it no longer evaporates or consumes away.</note>.

### CXXIII. What more is done with it? Do you turn it all into oil, or only a part?
**Answer:** One part is made "fixed" <note>To "fix" a substance means to make it solid and resistant to heat.</note>, and the other part is dissolved into oil.

### CXXIV. How do you make it fixed?
**Answer:** With its own <term>Antimony: Symbol ♁.</term> or fire, which does not burn the hands <note>original: *Feuer, das die Hände nicht verbrennt*; this refers to a "secret" or chemical heat, such as that produced by fermentation or a gentle digestive bath, rather than a literal flame.</note>.

### CXXV. What does the Stone tincture?
**Answer:** It is capable of tincturing <note>To "tincture" (tingiren) means to transform the internal properties of a base metal, "dyeing" it into gold or silver.</note> the whole world.

### CXXVI. Why should and can one not then perform the Great Work from common <term>Gold: Symbol ☉.</term> and Silver, since most people have made their Stone from <term>Gold: Symbol ☉.</term> and Silver?
**Answer:** Because it is not possible to make blossoms again from ripe fruits, or a child from a man; one cannot return him to his mother's womb to be born anew <note>This is a fundamental alchemical principle: common metals are "dead" or "fixed" by nature and cannot be easily regressed to their primal, generative state. The alchemist seeks the "Philosophical" metals which still possess their "growth" potential.</note>. But to Art it is permitted to promote the growth of the raw and unripe, to ennoble it and lead it to the highest stage, which Nature cannot do; but that many have made their stones from common <term>Gold: Symbol ☉.</term> and <term>Silver: Symbol ☽.</term> because they could not find the Great Work as the philosophical <term>Gold: Symbol ☉.</term> and <term>Silver: Symbol ☽.</term>, so they had to [use] the <term>Menstrua: Solvents or catalysts used to break down substances; from the Latin word for monthly cycles, implying a natural timing or flow.</term>, coloring wet and dry—
<meta>catchword: dry—</meta>

<summary>This page details the preparation of "Philosophical Antimony" into an incombustible oil and explains that the Great Work must begin with "unripe" materials rather than common gold or silver.</summary>
<keywords>Antimony, Incombustible Oil, Tincture, Great Work, Gold, Silver, Menstrua, Philosophical Metals</keywords>

...use dry spirits on common gold and silver, coloring them within and bringing them to a tincture, which tinctures much or little, according to how often they have been dissolved therein and brought into an oil.

### CXXVII. Can one also make tincturing Stones from minerals?

**Answer:** Yes, especially from <term>Antimony: Symbol ♁; in alchemy, this often refers to the "First Matter" or a purified mineral ore rather than the modern element.</term>, following both the wet and dry paths. When the <term>Virgin’s Milk: A symbolic name for a potent, white acidic solvent or "mercurial water" used to dissolve solids.</term> dissolves it, or our dry <term>Mercury: Symbol ☿; the "Philosophical Mercury" or the life-force/liquid principle of metals.</term> is united with it for seven nights, they both dissolve each other so that they become one and rise up like the beautiful <note>original: *Morgenröthe*; the "Dawn" or "Aurora," a color stage in the alchemical process where the matter turns reddish-purple, signaling the transition to the final stage.</note>Dawn. When one cleanses and separates this further, and "binds the wings" <note>To "bind the wings" of a substance means to "fix" it, or make a volatile, evaporating gas into a stable, heat-resistant solid.</note> of the predatory, volatile <term>Antimony: Symbol ♁.</term> so that it returns to its center, one will find that its thievish and predatory nature <note>Antimony was called the "Wolf of Metals" because it "devours" impurities in gold; here, the author suggests transforming this aggressive property into a creative one.</note> has been taken away, and it can clothe <term>Silver: Symbol ☽.</term> in a golden garment.

### CXXVIII. How can this water do such great things, that it can even ennoble raw and unripe minerals and transform them into magnificent tinctures?

**Answer:** Simple, ordinary water cannot do it; rather, it is our exalted, perfect water when it has become "heavenly." And as for its coloring poisons—these possess such poisonous virtue <note>In alchemy, "poison" often refers to the raw, transformative power of a substance that must be purified or "tamed" to become a medicine.</note> that they must ennoble themselves and be born anew when the Great Work is made from them. For we add nothing foreign to it except the elements, joining them back...

### CXXI. What is the <term>Fire: Symbol △; here representing the "Fire of Nature" or the internal Sulphur that drives growth and change.</term> of Nature?
**Answer:** It is our white <term>Fire: Symbol △.</term>.

### CXXII. What is done with it?
**Answer:** It must be dissolved into <term>Oil: In alchemy, an "oil" is a liquid state where a substance has been purified into its most concentrated, essence-like form.</term>, so that it becomes the incombustible oil. <note>original: *unverbrennliche Oehl*; a substance that is perfectly "fixed" and cannot be consumed or destroyed by physical fire.</note>

### CXXIII. What more is done with it? Is it all turned into oil, or only a part?
**Answer:** One part is made <term>Fixed: To "fix" a substance is to make it stable under heat so it no longer evaporates.</term>, and the other part is dissolved into oil.

### CXXIV. With what does one make it fixed?
**Answer:** With its own <term>Fire: Symbol △.</term> or heat, which does not burn the hands. <note>This refers to the "secret fire" of the alchemists—a gentle, metabolic heat rather than a literal flame.</note>

### CXXV. What does the Stone tincture?
**Answer:** It is capable of <term>Tincturing: The act of "tingeing" or transforming the internal nature of a base metal into a noble one.</term> the entire world.

### CXXVI. Why, then, should and can one not make the <term>Great Work: The "Magnum Opus," or the entire process of creating the Philosopher's Stone.</term> out of common <term>Gold: Symbol ☉.</term> and Silver, given that most people have made their Stone from <term>Gold: Symbol ☉.</term> and Silver?
**Answer:** For the reason that it is not possible to make blossoms again out of ripe fruit, or a child out of a man; one cannot bring him back into his mother's womb to be born anew. However, the <note>original: *der Kunst*; "the Art," meaning the practice of Alchemy.</note>Art is permitted to encourage the growth of the raw and the unripe, to ennoble it and lead it to the highest stage, which Nature cannot do. But because many could not find the Great Work as the "Philosophical" <term>Gold: Symbol ☉.</term> and <term>Silver: Symbol ☽.</term>, they made their Stones from common <term>Gold: Symbol ☉.</term> and <term>Silver: Symbol ☽.</term>; they were forced to use the <term>Menstrua: From the Latin "menstruum," referring to a powerful solvent used to dissolve solids into their primary components.</term>, coloring liquids, both wet and dry—

### CXXVII. Can one also make tincturing stones out of minerals?

...transformed. We bring [the]se things together and wait for them to yield fruit for us, because everything in both the <term>Great World: The macrocosm or the universe.</term> and the <term>Small World: The microcosm or the human being.</term> consists of the elements. We drive one sharp arrow <note>Symbolic of sharp solvents or the penetrating heat of the fire.</note> after another into it, raising the spirits as we see fit, especially in the <term>Multiplication: The stage where the power and quantity of the alchemical medicine is increased.</term> and <term>Illumination: A stage where the matter becomes bright and radiant.</term>, for there is not just one way, but many.

We paint white upon black, yellow upon white, red upon yellow, and even a deep purple color. The red purple mantle covers all other colors; for when the sun shines red and hot, the other stars in the sky are no longer seen because of the sun's brilliance. Thus, here too, the other planets and the moon are hidden under the sun's brilliance, so that they are no longer seen.

### CXXIX. What then is the Demogorgon?

**Answer:** The <term>Demogorgon: In alchemical mythology, a primeval deity representing the chaotic "first matter" of the universe or the "Ancient One" who contains all nature within himself.</term> is our Ancient One, who holds all of nature within himself; he is also our dragon. He possesses a central fire and vapor within him, namely the <term>Spirit of the World: original: *Spiritum mundi*; the universal life force or subtle essence that permeates all of creation.</term> or **Mercury** <note>Symbol: ☿</note>. This very spirit he releases from his heart; likewise, he provides a pure earth, which must be prepared with great skill.

### CXXX. What then is the fire against nature?

**Answer:** I have already said it: it is the corrosive, foul-smelling water, the <term>Stygian Water: original: *Aqua Stygia*; a powerful acidic solvent named after the River Styx in the underworld, used to dissolve or "kill" metallic bodies.</term>. In this water, the dragon is killed and the **Mercury** <note>Symbol: ☿</note> is <term>Fixed: original: *fix gemacht*; the process of turning a volatile, liquid, or vaporous substance into a solid that can withstand heat.</term>. This water is the scythe of the flying **Saturn** <note>Symbol: ♄; Saturn (representing lead and coldness) is often depicted with a scythe to show how he "cuts the wings" of the volatile Mercury to make it solid.</note>, with which he cuts off the wings of **Mercury** <note>Symbol: ☿</note>. It is the hellish fire that <term>Calcines: original: *calcinirt*; to reduce a substance to a dry powder or ash through intense heat.</term> and transforms it into a fixed body, turning the **Mercury of the Philosophers** <note>Symbol: ☿</note> into a stable **Precipitate**.

### CXXI. <note>The original text numbers this 121, though it follows 130.</note> Why should this fire fix the Mercury, when it was mentioned earlier that it should be hardened in the earth and coagulated therein?

**Answer:** Previously, the slaggy and watery **Mercury** <note>Symbol: ☿</note> had to be hardened by its own earth or salt through the "iron salt." For every earth has the power to harden its own **Mercury** <note>Symbol: ☿</note>. When it embraces such dry earth or salt, its moisture is dried up so that it is no longer flowing, but instead becomes numb, hardened, and <term>Coagulated: The process of a liquid thickening into a solid or semi-solid state.</term>. For it must unite with its own fixed body; therefore, it is called a **Mercury of the Bodies**. Likewise, it is "doubled" when it has made such a body volatile...

### CXXXIII. What do the <term>Adepts: original: *Adepti*; those who have mastered the secret of the Philosopher's Stone.</term> mean when they say one should conclude the work in the number ten; how is this to be understood?

**Answer:** It is to be understood thus: from our one single <term>Subject: original: *Subjecto*; the base matter or "Prima Materia" chosen for the work.</term>, two are made, namely Heaven and Earth; from these come the four <term>Elements: original: *Elementa*; Fire, Air, Water, and Earth.</term> and the three <term>Principles: original: *Principia*; Salt, Sulfur, and Mercury.</term>. Conjoin all of these, put them together, and conclude the work in the number ten, then it is perfect. 1: one thing; 2: Heaven and Earth; 3: the three Principles; 4: the four Elements. These numbers added together—1, 2, 3, 4—make ten.

Upon her head, she wears a crown of opal, and in her hand, she holds a little book of <term>Electrum: original: *Electro*; a precious natural alloy of gold and silver, often viewed by alchemists as having unique magical properties.</term>.

### CXXXVI. Who then has the key to this residence-house of <term>the Moon: original: *☽*</term>?

### CXXXVIII. ->What then is the name of the key that must unlock the residence-house of the Sun, and who possesses it?<-

### CXXXIX. ->Who can demand so many keys, or name or know them all? Is it not impossible?<-

He will surely hand over a certain key; once the first key is well applied and the first lock well opened, a treasure chest is found in which many magical keys lie.

### CXLI. But what of the Red?

**Answer:** This is dissolved in its own red oil; repeat this process until the <term>triangle</term> appears as oil.

### CXLII. How then is the composition of the Stone made?

**Answer:** One calcines <note>To "calcine" is to heat a substance to high temperatures in the presence of air to change its physical state, usually to a powder or glass.</note> the volatile <term>mercury: original: ☿</term> through the <note>original: ignis fortissimus</note> strongest fire, into a fixed glass which appears somewhat greenish because it is precipitated with the property of <term>Venus: original: ♀; the metal copper or the feminine planetary influence</term> or its "blood." This is the <term>horizontal gold: original: Aurum horizontale; a term for gold-in-potential or "terrestrial gold" that has not yet reached its highest "vertical" or celestial state</term>. It is brought to this state through its elevating and nourishing water of life, until the glass becomes meltable and fluid; it can even be brought into the form of an oil.

### CXLIII. This is however only the white stone; how is the red one made?

**Answer:** When this white fixed <term>triangle</term>—which takes the form of a bright, very heavy glass with a greenish tint and bright fractures—is dissolved in the white ferment, the red king in purple robes is then led to this white queen, so that she may hold a <note>original: Beylager; an archaic term for a ceremonial wedding night or the consummation of a marriage</note> wedding-bed with him and receive the solar son from him.

### CXLIV. Is the red stone now finished here?

**Answer:** Yes, it is finished, and it is a short path; but as for it being the deep-red, bloody stone—it is not that. It is the red tincture, the stone of the first order upon the red, and such [can...]

### CXLV. But in what manner is it brought higher?

**Answer:** Through its fruitful consort <note>The "consort" or "wife" refers to the complementary alchemical principle (often the solvent or the lunar Mercury) required to join with the "King" or "Sulfur" to increase its power.</note>, with whom it must often lie; for this Tincture must be dissolved several times in the red oil, or saturated with it, which is its fermentation.

### CXLVI. Are there not even more other paths and shortcuts?

**Answer:** There are very many; who could describe them all? I will, however, set down two here: one path for the White, and the other for the Red. 

1. For the White: one dissolves the white fixed <term>Sulfur: original: 🜍; the active, masculine principle of the work</term> in its white oil, as in its ferment, until it is also brought into the state of an oil. Then take <term>running mercury: original: ☿ currentem; common quicksilver</term>, as one might purchase it in a shop, and put it into a vessel. Pour this oil over it and keep it in the fire until it is fixed. This <term>precipitate: a solid that has settled out of a liquid during a chemical reaction</term> will transform a very large part of other ores—be it lead or <term>Venus: original: ♀; the metal copper</term>—into silver. 

In the same way, 2. one dissolves the red-fixed <term>Sulfur: original: 🜍</term> through its red ferment until it appears in the form of oil, and pours this oil onto <term>Mercury: original: ☿</term> which must have been placed in the vessel beforehand. Hold it likewise in the fire until it is fixed, and thus this precipitate will transform a very large part of other metals into <term>Gold: original: ☉; the Sun</term>.

### CXLVIII. But how is it made for the red?

### CXLIX. Since there are still better ways, I would like to know them as well?

**Answer:** Who can describe them all? The best ways are when one dissolves common <term>gold: original: ☉</term> and silver through the two <term>ferments: original: Fermenta; catalysts used to "leaven" or ripen the alchemical substance</term>—namely the Red and the White—and thereafter likewise joins together and conjugates the white and red tinctures in the red and white ferment, and then fixes them.

<meta>This page continues the discussion of alchemical preparations, specifically addressing the transition from potentially hazardous corrosive substances to refined medicinal "waters" or "oils." It follows the previous page's instruction on the best way to process precipitates.</meta>

-> * ):( o ):( * <-

...to boil and turn black, then one should not take it, for it does not serve for the <term>Work: the alchemical process of transformation</term>, but only to rinse out the vessels. How then can such sharp vinegar be the "waters of health"? Would they not surely eat the body apart—the stomach and the intestines?

**Answer:** Who wants to devour the elements of air and fire? Certainly, it is poison here, and yet the medicine must come from this poison through the <term>composition: original: Composition; the art of combining substances to balance their properties</term>, where one dragon and one poison must kill the other, so that a new life may grow from it. However, the elements are not without power and effect—with the exception of the "air" or "oil," which is a very great poison. Whoever used such a thing would be worse off than if he ate <term>basilisk eyes: a metaphor for a lethal, concentrated poison</term>. 

But the <term>phlegm: original: Plegma; a watery distillate</term> of <term>mercury: original: ☿</term>, which rises as a whitish mist and also in round droplets, is not poisonous. When a few drops are put into <term>spirit of wine: original: Spiritus vini; high-proof alcohol</term>, it becomes a <term>virgin’s milk: original: Jungfer-Milch; a milky medicinal suspension</term>. A dose of half or a whole spoonful causes a gentle vomiting because of its rawness—or rather because of its <term>mercurial: original: ☿alischen</term> nature, whose virtue works upwards because it is moist and airy. It is far better than a <term>pearl-drink: original: Perlen-Tranck; a luxury tonic made of dissolved pearls</term>, and indeed a pearl-drink can be made through it. 

It is good for <term>dropsy: original: Wassersucht; fluid retention</term>, <term>podagra: original: Podagra; gout of the foot</term>, <term>gout: original: Gicht</term>, <term>the aches: original: Zipperlein; an old term for joint pains</term>, the <term>rose: original: Rose; a skin infection, likely erysipelas</term>, and inflammation of the blood. It cools and extinguishes the <term>microcosmic fire: the internal heat or fever of the human body</term>, opens all <term>obstructions: original: Obstructiones; blockages of the bodily humors</term>, increases natural moisture, and cools the inflamed and heated liver. It is the true <term>water of Saturn: original: Aqua ♄; a cooling medicinal water associated with lead</term> and a mineral spring; it makes the heart and lungs fresh and is good against all swellings.

### CLI.
I would still like to be informed regarding the power of the elements: what nature, power, and taste each actually possesses in itself, as well as their effect and capability when these bear new fruits, and how, finally, through the complete composition, the sweet medicine is to be attained?

**Answer:** Firstly, our earth is made very fiery and dry; it is the constant star, <note>original: ignis & terra</note> fire and earth. This is then moistened with its water; it rages and seethes, roars and gives off bubbles, just as when one pours <term>spirit of antimony: original: Spiritum ♁</term> onto <term>salt of tartar: original: Sal tartari; potassium carbonate</term>. For this is the <note>original: agens und patiens</note> active and the passive agent; though they may have previously dwelled in one body, they are yet of opposing natures. And if these signs do not appear during the composition, then neither the water nor the earth has been rightly prepared. 

These two "kill" one another; the salt is dry and bitter, and very fiery, while the water is sour, yet internally sweet. When it runs through this earth, the desired <term>water of Saturn: original: Aqua ♄; lead acetate, known for its sweet taste</term> is produced. Through these two, a new life and a new taste emerge: our <term>Mercury of life: original: Lebens ☿</term>, which unites <note>original: radicaliter</note> at the very root with its body. 

It now moves out from the water or sea onto the dry land. There, no sharpness remains, nor any bitterness, but instead there is the Manna, the sweet bread of heaven, the Tree of Life, which increases the <term>radical moisture: original: Humidum radicale; the fundamental life-sustaining fluid in alchemy and medieval medicine</term>. It makes all trees fruitful, casts out gray hairs, pushes out [old] nails, and like the ice-

### CLII. How then does one use this Mercury of life?

**Answer:** One can use it in many ways in medicine: in lozenges, in sugar, and one can also dissolve it in <term>sweet spirit of salt: original: Spiritus Salis dulcis; a neutralized or sweetened form of hydrochloric acid</term>, pour it into dissolved sugar, allow it to gently evaporate into a salt, and use this salt daily. Likewise, [it can be prepared] in the volatile <term>spirit of urine: original: Spiritu 🜔; an ammoniacal solution</term>, letting this smoke off into a salt in dissolved sugar. Or, one can dissolve it in highly rectified <term>spirit of wine: original: Spiritu Vini; high-proof ethanol</term> and distill it into a pleasant-tasting and sweet-smelling <term>Star of Mercury: original: Astro Mercurii</term> until the whole substance has ascended. One can also use it before it has even sublimated into the <term>foliated earth: original: Terra foliata</term> <note>A chemical state where the substance forms leaf-like crystals, often referring to potassium acetate or a similar salt in this context</note> and is still the crystal of Mercury; then one boils this stone in oil, turpentine, almond oil, or olive oil, with which one only rubs the painful limbs externally. But whoever merely licks this stone for internal illnesses will become healthy. All wounds and injuries—be they as bad and evil as they may, even if all the flesh and veins have already rotted—it will nevertheless set everything right through the power of its salt. Its miraculous cures cannot be described: consumption, dropsy, gout, <term>podagra: original: Podagra; a form of gout specifically affecting the foot</term>, stroke,

### CLIII. But if this <term>Mercury: ☿</term> is precipitated and made fixed <note>In alchemy, "fixing" a substance means making a volatile or liquid material stable and resistant to heat</note>, does it then possess even greater virtue?

**Answer:** Without any doubt, for it receives much more power and capability through the <term>Stygian Water: original: Aqua Stygia</term> <note>A poetic name for a highly corrosive acid, named after the river Styx in the underworld</note>; because in this <term>Oil of Saturn: original: Oleo ♄</term> is contained <term>Venus: ♀</term> <note>copper or the feminine principle</note> and the spirit of the rising sun <note>gold or the masculine principle</note>. But because this is the smoking, deadly water that no one could drink, this <term>Lion: ♌</term> had to fight with the Eagle <note>The "Eagle" usually symbolizes volatility or acidic vapors, while the "Lion" represents the fixed or solid material; their struggle is a metaphor for a violent chemical reaction</note>. For the corrosive oil dissolves the <term>foliated earth: original: geblätterte Erde</term> <note>A crystalline state of the substance</note>, whereby its corrosiveness and poison are killed. 

Through this, they both lose their lives: the Eagle's wings are burned by this fire, and Mercury copulates with Venus. Then it rains gold <note>original: ☉</note> from the vapors that rise from these two bodies, and Venus, the <term>Green Lion: original: der grüne ♌</term> <note>A common alchemical term for raw vitriol or a specific mineral starter</note>, must endure a hard transformation <note>original: Transmutation</note>. Through this union, she must be changed into <term>Silver: ☽</term>, and our Mercury into <term>horizontal gold: original: Aurum horizontale</term> <note>A specialized medicinal gold supposed to work on the human "horizon" or physical plane</note>. 

Thus, this precipitate is certainly of much greater virtue, for now Mercury has stolen the treasure of Venus and robbed Saturn of his sun, which he kept guarded in his prison. He has set it free and clothed himself in Gold and Silver. Now both are without poison and are one body. The dose is very small, the effect powerfully great, and it is "all in all": the physician Raphael <note>The archangel associated with healing</note>, the highest medicine, the treasure of wealth and health. ### CLIV. How does one use this in medicine?

**Answer:** One can use it in wine, in beer, or in <term>Spirit of Wine: original: Spiritu Vini</term> <note>High-proof alcohol or ethanol</note>, also in a confection <note>A medicinal sugar-based preparation</note>.

### CLV. a. Is that which is here called "Spirit of Wine" actually the <term>Water of Life: original: Aqua-Vit</term> of the Stone—its Oil, which consists of Body, Soul, and Spirit, and is also called the "Air"?

**Answer:** No, by no means! This oil is poison and death, for the Stone is dyed a very deep color by this poison.

### CLVI. b. So does the **Red Stone** <note>The final, most potent stage of the alchemical Great Work</note> not serve for medicine because this poisonous oil is added to it?

**Answer:** The **Red Stone** is the greatest and most heavenly medicine, and this poisonous oil gives it its greatest powers; the highest medicine must be made out of this **Serpent**. The greatest cures come from the poisonous **Arsenic** itself; yet this <term>Basilisk</term> <note>A mythical reptile whose gaze could kill; in alchemy, it represents a raw, lethal state of matter that must be "tamed"</note> must be killed and no longer be alive. Therefore, a bright mirror is prepared for it so that it kills itself <note>A reference to the myth where a basilisk dies upon seeing its own reflection; alchemically, this implies a process of self-neutralization or "reflection" within the sealed vessel</note>, so that one can see through the poisonous heart of this Basilisk. 

Therefore, it is well to note that when the Stone is yellow, it is not used in medicine. Here it changes its taste once more: previously it was bitter, but through the <term>Oil of Arsenic: original: Oleum Arsenici</term> it becomes sweet. The smell is loathsome, hot, and dry; the colors are mostly yellow and sky-blue; it receives anew the name of the **Poisonous Dragon**.

### CLVI. If he then does not serve as medicine, what is done with him?

**Answer:** One must kill this poisonous **Dragon** through a fire which has the power to alter his poison and transform it into a healing medicine, so that this poisonous **Salamander** <note>A mythical lizard believed to live in and endure fire; here it represents the "fixed" substance that does not evaporate when heated</note> can live in the fire; he does not burn therein, but only sheds his poison. When he is killed, he is made alive through his own blood, just as the **Phoenix** <note>A bird that is consumed by fire and reborn from its own ashes; a symbol of the "Red Work" or final stage of alchemy</note> is burned through the fire, and the **Pelican** <note>In alchemy, the "Pelican" is a specific type of circulating flask; symbolically, it refers to the bird believed to feed its young with its own blood, representing the "nourishing" of the Stone</note> sprinkles his dead young with blood.

### CLVII. How does one kill this dragon?

**Answer:** **Phoebus** <note>A name for Apollo, the Sun; represents the alchemical "Solar Fire" or the gold principle</note> shoots his arrows into him. His whole body is blue and yellow from poison; this is seen in his breath when he lies in his cave and exhales his poisonous vapor. But **Phoebus** lurks for him and shoots his rays—his fiery arrows—into him. This **Solar-Fire** and heat make him completely mad and raging; he wishes to flee, but the more he flees, the more **Phoebus** shoots arrows into him until his whole body is wounded and begins to bleed. In this blood, he becomes a medicine, and his whole body must melt into pure blood. This **Dragon's Blood** is the treasure of the world, the treasure of wealth and health. Whoever finds this key to the dragon's cave—so that he can let the **Sun** in to fight this dragon—has won; for he has overcome the two enemies of human life: sickness and poverty. He has fled these enemies,

### CLVIII. What is most important to observe in this work, or what special secrets are there in the preparation of this work, and what are the signs of its years and maturity?

**Answer:** The first secret is the maturity of **Mercury** <note>represented by the symbol ☿; in this context, it refers to the "Philosophic Mercury," the essential spirit or solvent used to create the Stone</note>, for one must well know the birthplace of the **Stone**, over which one must watch day and night; whoever does not know this will lose everything. For it will break of its own accord, and this earth will crush itself, and this stone will shatter so that it yields flour. As soon as the **Capricorn** <note>original: *Stein-Bock*, literally "Stone-Buck." This is an alchemical pun: *Stein* means stone, and the zodiac sign Capricorn marks the winter solstice, a period associated with the "birth" or crystallization of the matter in the flask</note> is born, the hunters must seize it, otherwise it cannot be caught; it departs and leaves behind an empty nest.

### CLIX. But how does one catch it?

**Answer:** I have considered this in my little treatise titled *The Mineral Glows* <note>original: *Mineralische Gluten*; "Gluten" can also refer to a sticky, binding substance or heat/embers</note>, which I will not repeat here to avoid tediousness, but will provide this information in abundance: when those signs appear, take some of it and cast it upon a glowing plate. If it all vanishes in smoke, then sublimate <note>To heat a substance so it turns to vapor and then becomes solid again; a primary method of purification</note> it yourself, and do not wait until this earthquake occurs. Thus one is without danger and can set the proper snare <note>original: *Leimruthe*, literally a "lime-twig," a traditional tool coated in sticky birdlime to catch birds</note> for this bird.

### CLX. What signs are there of maturity in the White Stone?

**Answer:** The **sea of glass** <note>A common alchemical term for the molten or liquid state of the matter inside the glass vessel, often appearing reflective</note> must often provide a smooth mirror before it fully hardens. It will frequently become hard; however, if one were to try to break it open then, thinking this "stony palace" was already built, it would not mix with the **Oil**, and it would also extinguish the fire. Instead, one must allow it its proper years. It will melt and solidify many times until it finally achieves its correct hardness. Then, this glass shatters on its own, so that many a man—out of fear and the expectation of things yet to come—runs out of the room in terror, not knowing what has happened to him. Finally, he returns and finds his **sea of glass** shattered into nothing but **diamonds**, coarse, small, and angular, like coarsely crushed crystals; this is the true, coarse **Sea-Salt of Nature**. I know of a good friend whose work shattered into nothing but <term>square</term> <note>original: □; the square or cubic symbol signifies stable, physical form or salt</note> stones, as if they had been polished. This diamond must then be softened.

### CLXI. What danger or fear is there regarding the Red Stone?

**Answer:** I have already considered this in my other small treatise, the **Philosophical Pearl-Tree**, which I likewise do not wish to repeat here. However, I will mention this in addition: one must not give it more fire than it can endure, otherwise its entire body becomes a **Fire-Spirit** and returns into its **Chaos**. For it loves the **Sun’s fire** <note>In alchemy, the "Sun's fire" refers to a high, intense heat or the solar quality of Gold/Sulfur</note> so greatly, and when it is ignited too intensely by it...

For this Stone undergoes many changes; it is made <term>volatile</term> <note>original: flüchtig; able to be vaporized by heat</note> many times, then <term>fixed</term> <note>original: fix; resistant to the fire</note>, then volatile again, and then fixed once more. ### CLXII. **Is it then true that the correct <term>Materia</term> <note>The raw "Matter" or starting substance of the Great Work</note> is to be found in sufficient quantities in all shops everywhere, but is unregarded and cast aside as a lowly and worthless thing?**

**Answer:** Yes, in former times it was available for purchase in all shops, a pound for 6 pence; but now only in its smelted form, not its <term>Minera</term> <note>The raw ore or mineral state as it comes from the earth</note>. Yet the smelted material is useless for the Work. Previously, the <term>Minera</term> was so worthless that no one used it, and this stone was "thrown after the cow" <note>A German idiom meaning the item was treated as utter junk or barnyard waste</note>. But once its nature became known, the Enemy of the Human Race <note>A common historical reference to the Devil, or perhaps here, personified greed</note> snatched it out of the hands of men, and it was sold to Holland and Italy, where a monopoly was made of it. Afterward, however, people in **Germany** also learned how to treat this **Materia**, though only as a lowly, trifling thing, and likewise created a monopoly out of it, so that almost no one can buy it publicly, even though the whole country is full of it in the mountains

[...wealth] and health made common to all; that is God's will, and the time of **Revelation** <term>Offenbahrung</term> is at hand. This will and impulse of God must be followed and fulfilled, for at all times the **innocent** <term>Unmündigen</term> <note>literally "those not of age," referring to the simple-hearted or those without formal status</note> and the **unlearned masses** <term>ungelehrte Hauffe</term> have possessed the grace of being God’s dearest friends. Also, after His resurrection, the Son of God first revealed Himself to a **woman** <note>original: Weibs-Person</note>, thereby showing from the beginning of the world that He has chosen all that was despised, foolish, and held as unlearned by the world. He does not let Himself be bound to the **worldly scholars** <term>Welt-Gelehrten</term>, but rather His Spirit is the true teacher and the "high school" where all worldly scholars must fall silent. They may dispute until the end of the world and yet accomplish nothing, seeking all science and foundation only in the "sound of words," while letting the inner foundation go; they bite at the shells but never taste the kernel. 

Let a person take this as a total certainty: if he stands well in the **outer heaven** and is looked upon with favor, then the **inner heaven** <term>innerer Himmel</term> <note>a mystical concept referring to the spiritual realm within the soul that mirrors the cosmos</note> is even more favorable to him. He has the hope, through a godly and virtuous life, to draw near to God and explore His secrets, whether he be woman or man,

<summary>The author argues that spiritual and alchemical secrets are not the property of worldly scholars, but are revealed by God to the humble, the unlearned, and women, citing biblical examples like Miriam and Deborah.</summary>

**CLXIV.** But why should there be only this single material in all of nature from which this high work is to be made, since there are so many magnificent minerals that have pure, valid <term>Antimony</term> <note>original symbol: ♁; here referring to the "star regulus" or the base mineral essence</note>, and some even contain <term>Gold</term> <note>original symbol: ☉</note> which was volatile? Surely these would be better for the task than this leaden essence?

**Answer:** Materials that contain <term>Gold</term> <note>original symbol: ☉</note> are all the worse for it. What is the gold supposed to do? It possesses no **tingeing spirit** <note>a substance capable of "tinging" or transforming the color and nature of a base metal</note> like our material does. In our material, even the raw spirit performs the tingeing; its **white sublimate** <note>a solid deposit produced by heating a substance into a vapor and letting it cool</note>, which it releases by itself, colors <term>Copper</term> <note>original symbol: ♀</note> a beautiful white. Likewise, it turns <term>Iron</term> <note>original symbol: ♂</note> into glass if one allows them both to flow in a gentle fire. No other mineral body can do this—to bring metals to the final "glass-degree" in such a short time and penetrate to the very heart of the metals. Now, what shall this **bird** <note>an alchemical term for a volatile substance that "flies" or evaporates when heated</note> do when it returns to its center, unites with it, and becomes a **fixed glass**? Previously, when it was alone and still volatile and poisonous, it could barely color a tenth part; but once it is **born anew**, it does so with many thousand-fold power. No raw spirit of the metals can do this; it cannot spread itself out in such a way.

**CLXV.** This must surely be the **tingeing spirit** of which the Philosophers write: <note>original Latin: Arsenicum esse Tincturam Albedinis, und nicht Rubedinis</note> "**Arsenic is the Tincture of Whiteness, and not of Redness.**" But this is surely no common arsenic?

**CLXVI.** Previously it was mentioned that when the **white Tincture** <note>a substance capable of transforming base metals into silver</note> has become a "glassy sea," it shatters of its own accord and breaks the glass with a great crash. How does this happen?

Likewise in the **Glass of Saturn** <term>original: Vitro ♄; lead-based glass</term>. When these cool down, notice how they jump and crackle and crash, dividing themselves into splinters. Why should it not also be so in this work, when its fire departs from it?

**CLXVII.** With what kind of fire, however, is this burned to the "glass-degree"?

**Answer:** Through the fire of nature—its own internal fire.

Regarding the rose-color: if the solution does not wish to take on the color well, one should boil it until it is dry, while stirring it constantly. When it has become completely cold, it turns rose-colored. If it is warmed again, it returns to grass-green, then bright sky-blue, then violet, and finally rose-red—which is its final, stable color. It also appears as pale blue, violet-blue, and in various dark and pale shades. One can demonstrate these colors to others if desired; however, beyond being a delight to witness, they also have their practical use. 

If one allows it to evaporate to dryness in a **water bath** <term>Latin: balneum; a vessel of hot water used to heat another container gently and evenly</term> and then pours highly rectified **spirit of wine** <note>original: "Wein-Geist"; high-proof alcohol or ethanol</note> over it—the longer it stands, the better it becomes—it will then **tint** <term>Latin: tingere; to color or imbue</term> itself and absorb the subtle essence. Just as when **gold** <term>represented by the symbol ☉</term> is dissolved by these **salts** <term>Latin: salia</term>, this substance possesses a power that far surpasses the contents of common apothecary jars.

### CLXX. Since this red sulfur <term>original symbol: △; Sulphur</term> <note>The "sulfur" mentioned here likely refers to an alchemical principle of combustibility or a specific mineral extract rather than common brimstone</note> is so dry and hot, how can it be burned into glass?

**Answer:** This happens through its own spirit and fire, which can also give it "flux" <note>the ability to melt and flow like a liquid</note>; however, I will repeat the preparations <note>original: Medicamenta</note> here. One dissolves **salt** <term>original symbol: ⊖; Sal</term> in rainwater and adds an equal amount of **oil of vitriol** <note>original: Oleum Vitrioli; concentrated sulfuric acid</note>. Distill this over, and a **spirit of salt** <note>original: Spiritus Salis; hydrochloric acid</note> rises, while the liquid *sal enixum* <term>potassium bisulfate, a byproduct of this acid production</term> remains behind. Take 1 part of this and 2 parts of very finely powdered mineral; mix them well and apply fire for several hours. The glass **retort** <term>a glass vessel with a long neck used for distilling</term> must be broken to retrieve the contents. 

Take the **sublimate** <term>the solid substance that collected at the top of the vessel during heating</term> and grind it small—including whatever passed over into the receiver—and place everything back onto the *caput mortuum* <term>Latin: "dead head"; the chemical residue or dregs left after distillation</term>. Pour the spirit of salt, which was driven over from the *sal enixum*, onto it. Let it **digest** <term>to steep or soften in gentle heat</term> until it has turned rose-colored; in the fire, it is always green. 

Pour off the rose-colored solution <note>original: "Solution"</note> and distill it in a **water bath** <term>Latin: balneum</term>. Because some of it will want to pass over, this must be done in a low-necked flask. Afterward, it is distilled like a **spirit of vitriol** <note>original: Spiritus Vitrioli; a strong acid</note>. The spirit that passed over is drawn off together with the sublimate; because it passes over white, one dissolves the salt in this white liquid. This is the salt that has been boiled out of the *caput mortuum* with rainwater and evaporated back into a salt. 

Allow it to evaporate gently until the salt is dry. Upon this, pour the colored spirits and keep it in the fire until it has become **fixed** <term>stable in heat; no longer evaporating or changing when glowing</term> and does not smoke even when glowing red. This beautiful, wax-like flowing stone or salt possesses great powers in medicine <note>original: Medic.</note>. There is almost no ailment, whether a natural or unnatural sickness, that one cannot...

### CLXXI. Can one then not also treat this matter with **saltpeter** <term>original symbol: 🜔; Salniter</term>?

**Answer:** Yes. Take 3 or even 4 parts saltpeter and this mineral, ground very small. Hold it in the fire; it will not ignite the saltpeter, but rather it flows with an **ebullition** <note>original: Ebullition; a bubbling or boiling effect</note> because the acid of the saltpeter is "killed" <note>neutralized</note> by this **alkali** <note>original: Alcale</note>. It must not have a strong coal fire, otherwise it will boil over; also, no coals must fall into it, so that the saltpeter does not ignite. After 2 or 3 hours—depending on whether one has much or little—the saltpeter becomes **fixed** <term>rendered stable and non-volatile in the fire</term> in the process, and it becomes a sky-blue stone. 

This can be extracted with spirit of wine, or dissolved in the **spirit of the solvent** <note>The original text uses an asterisk (*), which the author's notes suggest refers to a specific prepared solvent or spirit</note> until it has become very blue. Draw the spirit over <note>distill it off</note>; onto what remains, one can pour spirit of wine, mix the extract with the spirit, or drive it over into colors as one pleases. This medicine is particularly excellent for brain diseases: for **melancholic** <note>depressed or despondent</note> or even mad and foolish people, for the **evil misery** <note>original: böse Noth; a historical term for severe epilepsy or fits</note> and **falling sickness** <note>epilepsy</note>, because here the **spirit of the Moon** <term>original symbol: 🜄 Lunæ; referring to the silver-principle or "spirit" associated with the Moon</term> is unlocked and predominates.

### CLXXII. But how is it treated by means of **antimony** <term>original symbol: 🜔; stibium</term>? <note>Note: The symbol 🜔 was used for saltpeter on the previous page, but here refers to antimony or an antimony-based preparation.</note>

### CLXXIII. Can one not also treat it with **aqua fortis** <term>original: AF; nitric acid</term>?

**Answer:** Yes, it is dissolved in aqua fortis. Once the solution has occurred and the **sediment** <note>the solid matter that settles at the bottom of the liquid</note> has settled, the rose-colored solution is poured off. Dissolve **antimony** <term>original symbol: 🜔</term> in as little water as possible, pour the rose-colored aqua fortis into it, and draw it off <note>evaporate it</note> until it becomes a dry salt in the **sand bath** <note>original: im Sande; a container of sand used to distribute heat evenly</note>. A rose-colored salt remains behind; this can be digested with spirit of wine, or perhaps infused with something; the salt must be ground very small.

### CLXXIV. Is there then no medicine to be made from it by the **dry path** <term>alchemical processes using high heat or melting rather than liquid solvents</term>?

**Answer:** Yes, if one melts it with an equal amount of **sulphur** <term>original symbol: 🜍</term> and **salt of tartar** <note>original: Sale tartari; potassium carbonate</note>. No coals must fall into it, otherwise it will reduce into a **regulus** <term>original: Könige; the metallic mass that settles at the bottom of a crucible</term>. Keep it in a steady flow for 6 hours, pour it into a warmed mortar, and while it is still warm, crush it very small. Pour strong spirit of wine upon it and let the **tincture** <term>an alcoholic medicinal extract</term> be extracted until it is very red. The power of this tincture is to purify the blood, to heal internal ulcers, to kill the **acid** <term>original: Acidum</term> in the stomach and intestines, and the twitching and jolting of the

It causes neither vomiting nor bowel movements, unless one were to eat it in great quantities, which is not necessary. Furthermore, this salt serves for all open sores that rot and stink, whether they be neglected, or are **erysipelas** <note>original: Rothlauffen; a bacterial skin infection also known as St. Anthony's Fire</note>, **swollen ulcers** <note>original: Oehl-Schenckel; literally 'oil-shanks', likely referring to weeping edema or chronic leg ulcers</note>, **canker** <note>original: Krebs; can refer to cancer or spreading sores</note>, and **lupus** <note>original: Wolff; an 'eating' ulcer or skin lesion</note>, or other wounds that refuse to heal; thus this is a masterly enough remedy for swelling and hot inflammation. It is also excellent when mixed into salves and plasters for nasty, evil wounds and **leprosy** <note>original: Aussatz</note>.

### CLXXV. Can one then not treat this material with **mercury** <term>original symbol: ☿; Mercurius</term>?

**Answer:** If one mixes this **subject** <note>the base material</note> with three times its weight of **corrosive sublimate** <note>original: corrisivischen Sublimat; mercuric chloride, a highly toxic but prized chemical in early medicine</note> and drives it immediately in an open fire, it yields a **spirit** and a red oil, which—contrary to the nature of other oils—sinks to the bottom in the spirit; if one [treats] such...

### CLXXVI. But how is it treated, and what is its use with common mercury?

**Answer:** This **ore** <term>original: Minera</term> is ground small, mixed with 2 or 3 parts of **mercury** <term>☿</term>, and distilled over. Thus the **mercury** <term>☿</term> increases from it and absorbs the **silver** <term>original symbol: ☽; Luna</term> and **antimony** <term>original symbol: ♁</term> from it. The more often this is done, the better. This can be seen when one **dissolves** <term>solvirt</term> such **mercury** <term>☿</term> in **aqua fortis** <term>original: AF.; nitric acid, a powerful solvent</term>; it will not want to dissolve easily because it has become "fat" <note>enriched or heavy with metallic content</note> from the process. Finally, the acid attacks it and it becomes sky-blue. This **mercury** <term>☿</term> can be **amalgamated** <term>alloyed</term> with **copper** <term>original symbol: ♀; Venus</term> and afterwards dissolved in **aqua fortis** <term>AF.</term>. When the **mercury** <term>☿</term> **digests** <term>is heated gently</term> with it and is drawn over again, other **gold** <term>original symbol: ☉; Sol</term> pyrites and ores can also be enriched with it.

### CLXXVII. What then is the *Liquor Alcahest*, about which so much is written and disputed?

**Answer:** There is much dispute regarding the **Liquor Alcahest** <note>A hypothetical "universal solvent" sought by alchemists, capable of dissolving any substance into its primary liquid form without altering its internal properties</note> of **Paracelsus** <note>Philippus Aureolus Theophrastus Bombastus von Hohenheim (1493–1541), the Swiss physician and alchemist who popularized the term Alcahest</note>. Many consider it to be the **solvent** <term>original: Menstruum</term> which is made from the volatile **salt of tartar** <term>original: Weinstein-Salz; potassium carbonate</term>...

...been made into salts, which I, however, do not believe for many reasons, because this only separates the **antimony** <term>original symbol: ♁; a brittle metalloid often used to purify gold</term> <note>original: Antimonii</note> from metals and releases the **mercury** <term>original symbol: ☿</term> <note>original: Mercurium</note>. However, the **Alcahest Liquor** <term>a hypothetical universal solvent sought by alchemists, capable of reducing any body to its primary matter</term> dissolves all metallic bodies into a salt; such a salt can never again be reduced into a metal throughout eternity, but instead dissolves in the air into water. This water, once it has been **putrefied** <term>the process of allowing a substance to decay or ferment in a gentle heat to release its essence</term> for its proper duration, allows the **quintessence** <note>the "fifth essence," the purest and most medicinal part of a substance</note> to separate at the top like an oil. This liquor is also called the **hellish fire** <note>original: höllische Feuer; likely referring to "Ignis Gehennae," a term for an acidic solvent that acts as powerfully as fire but without external heat</note>. It dissolves all stone, bone, glass, and sand, bringing everything into the form of a salt. It makes all volatile bodies **fixed** <note>resistant to evaporation or the effects of fire</note>, and all fixed bodies volatile, through the process of **cohobation** <term>the repeated distillation of a liquid by pouring it back onto the solid residue left in the vessel</term>. 

One example: **mercury** <term>☿</term> <note>original: Mercurius</note> becomes fixed through this process and gains a scent like **musk** <note>original: Biesam; a strong, earthy perfume used here to describe a chemical odor</note>. Through cohobation, it passes over in crystal-drops, which can again be brought into two distinct liquors, and can never again become **mercury** <term>☿</term>. Even the **Philosopher’s Stone** <note>original: Lapis Philosophorum; the ultimate goal of alchemy, used to transmute metals and heal all diseases</note> cannot overcome it. 

The wonders that can be performed through the **Specific Corrosive** in the mineral kingdom—as well as in the other two <note>referring to the animal and vegetable kingdoms</note>—are unspeakable. One can joyfully obtain the quintessence of all bodies without great effort; one need only melt the substances within it and draw the liquor from them. The salt that remains behind can be brought back into a metal by no art, but rather dissolves in the air into water. If this water is putrefied, the quintessence [appears] as a cream or fat at the top

### CLXXVIII. How is it made?

Answer. Take salt <term>🜔</term> <note>Common salt or sea salt</note> calcined until it is yellow, and the best vitriol <term>🜕</term> <note>sulfate minerals, usually of iron or copper, used to produce strong acids</note>, an equal portion of each. From these, a nitric acid <term>AF</term> <note>Aqua Fortis</note> is distilled without the addition of water. One dissolves the residue <term>Caput Mortuum</term> in distilled wine-vinegar to extract its salt, lets it evaporate to dryness, then pours the nitric acid over it and draws it off strongly from its salt until the retort <note>a glass vessel with a long neck used for distillation</note> glows. Into one pound of this nitric acid drawn from its residue, throw 4 loth <note>a historical unit of weight, roughly 15 grams or half an ounce</note> of corrosive sublimate and 3 loth of sal ammoniac <term>⚹</term> <note>ammonium chloride</note>. Distill it over, so that the sal ammoniac and sublimate are combined into a magnificent solvent <term>Menstruum</term>. Into this, pour an equal amount of oil of mercury <term>Oleum ☿</term> and distill it over again; then it is ready and always for use. This possesses very great powers,

### CLXXIX. I would also like to hear about the Corallate of Paracelsus, with which he is said to have performed so many miracle cures?

Answer. He describes it himself in this way: That you take the Liquor of the Eagle <note>original: "Liquorem Aquilae"; in alchemy, the "Eagle" often refers to sal ammoniac or a volatile mercurial spirit</note> and dry it out. Afterward, take the Liquor of prepared Antimony <term>♁</term> <note>a metalloid used in both medicine and the purification of metals</note>, moisten the first substance with it, and just as the process for calcined tartar <note>potassium carbonate</note> is to draw off its spirit, it is done the same way here, until the spirit is completely withdrawn: and thus the coral-matter is prepared. Here, by "Liquor of the Eagle," he means the wet virgin mercury <term>☿</term> <note>naturally occurring or highly pure quicksilver</note>, which should be dried and then doused with the liquor of antimony <term>♁</term>. This is done repeatedly until the substance remains entirely upon it and has become coral-red. Only those who have learned to separate the elements in the "philosophical work"—those who can prepare the mercury <term>☿</term> of the wise—can perform this. However, because he also taught how to make another "Corallate," I will include that one as well; it is, however, a description of a precipitate for the French Disease <note>original: "Morbo Gallico"; a historical term for syphilis</note> and other nasty illnesses. He describes it with these words: The mercury should be calcined in an *Aqua Fortis* <note>nitric acid; literally "strong water"</note>, then a graduating water <note>a solution used to "grade" or improve the color and purity of a metal</note> is drawn off from it once or several times until the precipitate takes on a pleasing and beautiful red color; then a rectified brandy <note>highly distilled alcohol or "spirit of wine"</note> is drawn off from it.

### CLXXX. The Circulated Salt; what is this?
**Answer:** It is the double salt of the Sages, and their **Mercury of the Philosophers** <term>Mercurius Philosophorum</term>, the **foliated earth** <note>original: "geblätterte Erde"; a stage in the alchemical work where the matter takes on a flaky, leaf-like appearance</note>,

But whoever cannot make this should use the sweet **crystals** from common salt; this is allowed to flow in a **retort** <note>a glass flask with a long, downward-pointing neck used for distillation</note>, behind which is a tube. In this tube, several drops of water are allowed to fall through a quill, and immediately the **spirits** <note>volatile vapors or essences</note> come forth with great violence. This is continued until all the salt has risen over. This is then **dephlegmated** <note>purified by removing the watery "phlegm"</note>; upon this, one pours **spirit of wine** <term>Spiritum vini</term> <note>high-proof alcohol</note>, draws it off, and **cohobates** <note>repeatedly redistills a liquid over its solid residue to concentrate its power</note> it 3 or 4 times. This is done as often as necessary until all the salt has risen over, and then it is **dephlegmated**. When this is left to stand in a cellar for a time, it forms into **crystals**. However, it is not that, for the **green Mercury** comes from another land—namely, the metallic salt, the Water-Stone of the Wise. From common table salt, a **Sal enixum** <note>original: "Sal enixum"; a neutral salt resulting from the reaction of an acid and an alkali</note> can also be made in this way: **Salt** <term>⊖</term> <note>Salt symbol</note> is **dissolved** in rainwater, into which is poured exactly as much **oil of Antimony** <term>Oleum ♁</term> <note>Antimony symbol; "oil" refers to a concentrated liquid form</note> as the salt weighed. This is drawn off, and the **Sal enixum** remains behind. With this, one can **dissolve** Gold <term>☉</term> <note>Gold symbol</note>, or golden **crocus** <term>Crocos</term> <note>a red or orange powder produced by heating a metal</note>. When it has flowed well with it, one pours it out and extracts the red salt with **spirit of wine**. This can be made into a flowable **alkali salt** <term>Sale alcali</term> if good **tincturing** **Antimony** <term>♁</term> is added to it and allowed to flow in the fire for 3 hours for **fixation** <note>the process of making a substance stable so it does not evaporate or change when heated</note>. But without **Antimony**, it must be made alkaline with coals if it is to **dissolve** Gold <term>☉</term> alone—

### CLXXXI. I would also like to know how to make Salt of Tartar volatile?

**Answer:** This happens in various ways; the first, which pleases me best, happens in this manner: A very fiery **salt of tartar** <note>potassium carbonate</note> is **dissolved in the air** <note>original: "solvirt"; the salt is allowed to absorb atmospheric moisture until it liquefies, a process called deliquescence</note>. Upon this is poured **volatile oil of turpentine** in a wide glass jar, and it is stirred together often. Thus, the fatty oil of turpentine takes the fiery salt into itself, and from both, a thick soap is formed—one oil dissolves the other. When they have become one "body" and no more moisture is visible, one distills it. Then the volatile **oil of turpentine** <note>original: "Oleum Terebintini"</note> rises over, and because it has united itself in its innermost being with the oil (that is, the salt of tartar) over the course of 40 days, it carries the salt up with it. This volatile salt of tartar can be increased **infinitely** <note>original: "in infinitum"</note> every day; if one takes 4 or 5 parts of this spirit, dissolves 1 part of salt of tartar in it, and draws it off, it multiplies itself. The other way to make salt of tartar volatile happens thus: **distilled wine vinegar** is poured over it, so that a tasteless water passes over, which

### CLXXXII. What use is the volatile salt of tartar, the spirit?

**Answer:** It has this benefit: it kills all corrosives and all acids present in humans that cause illness, pain, and **stabbing aches** <note>original: "Reissen"; likely referring to rheumatic or neuralgic pain</note> which waste away the body. It also breaks the stone in the kidneys and bladder. In cases of **gout** <note>original: "Podagra"</note>, arthritis, and **joint-aches** <note>original: "Zipperlein"</note>, it has no equal; it dissolves and melts the **tartar** <note>In paracelsian medicine, "tartar" referred to stony metabolic deposits in the body, comparing them to the crusts found in wine barrels</note>, especially when the spirit of **quicklime** <note>original: "ungelöschten Kalck"</note> is incorporated with it. It is also effective against corrosive poison that a person has taken into the body, whether it be a **corrosive sublimate** <note>mercuric chloride</note> or **strong water** <note>original: "Aqua fort"; nitric acid</note>, and also against **arsenic**—though the poison of arsenic is only killed by drinking milk, or likewise by butter, as I know of examples where people were saved by this, even though they had taken a great deal of it. This spirit is also good for the liver and spleen; when these are **hardened** <note>original: "scirrhofisch"; referring to a scirrhus or hard, tumorous mass</note> and toughened by such matter, it will soften and expel this stony material. Upon the pains of gout it will...

### 183. Can this oil or volatile spirit of tartar not serve the benefit of metals?

Answer: Yes, indeed; it performs pure miracles therein because, after the Universal Key, it is the second secret key. Firstly, in the entire vegetable kingdom, it allows all the powers of herbs, flowers, and roots, raisins, and all species to be brought into their **quintessence** <term>Quint-Essenz</term> <note>The 'fifth essence' or the purest, most concentrated medicinal part of a substance, separated from its raw physical matter</note>, while the coarse, earthy body is cast down to the bottom. But it also has a great affinity for the metallic kingdom and brings benefit to those who know how to apply this key well. It takes the **sulfur** <term>🜍</term> <note>In alchemy, sulfur represents the 'soul' of a substance, responsible for its color and flammability</note> from them and makes the **mercury** <term>☿</term> <note>The symbol for Mercury represents the 'spirit' or the principle of fluidity and volatility</note> loose and free. Likewise, it removes the poison from raw minerals. When a good, gold-like **sulfur** <term>🜍</term> has become a blood-red oil within it and **silver** <term>☽</term> <note>The symbol for Luna, representing silver</note> is digested with it, the silver can become true gold, which no **parting water** <note>original: "Scheide-Wasser"; a term for nitric acid or *aqua fortis*, used by alchemists to separate gold from silver</note> will ever attack. It also provides benefits for **mercury** <term>☿</term> and **lead** <term>♄</term> <note>The symbol for Saturn, representing lead</note>.

### CLXXXIII. Can this oil or volatile spirit of tartar not also serve for the benefit of metals?

Answer: Yes, certainly; it performs nothing but miracles therein because, after the Universal Key, it is the other secret key—primarily in the entire vegetable kingdom. By it, all the powers of herbs, flowers, and roots, raisins, and all species can be brought into their **quintessence** <term>Quint-Essenz</term> <note>The "fifth essence," or the purest and most concentrated medicinal force of a substance, separated from its physical bulk</note>, and the coarse terrestrial body can thereby be driven to the ground <note>Meaning the physical sediment is separated from the medicinal spirit</note>. Likewise, it removes the poison from raw minerals. When a good gold-like **sulfur** <term>🜍</term> within it has become volatile and turned into a blood-red oil, and **silver** <term>☽</term> is digested with it, it can become true gold, which no **parting-water** <term>Scheide-Wasser</term> <note>Nitric acid, used by early chemists to separate silver from gold by dissolving the silver</note> will ever attack; it also provides benefit with **mercury** <term>☿</term> and **lead** <term>♄</term>.

### CLXXXV. From what, then, is the animal stone made?

Answer: From the great animal creature, the human being, and **phosphorus** <note>In the 17th and 18th centuries, phosphorus was famously isolated from human urine, linking it directly to the "animal" kingdom in alchemy.</note> as well; these have great use in medicine, and many curious things are to be made with them; both can be used for the mineral kingdom.

### CLXXXVI. Do these stones then not serve for the great Universal Stone, or do they not belong to it?

Answer: One must not mix or mingle one with the other; like stays with like, and brings forth fruit according to its kind. Whoever acts against this creates a monstrosity and begets a mule <note>The author uses the "Maul-Esel" (mule) as a metaphor for a "sterile" alchemical result that occurs when a practitioner incorrectly mixes substances from different kingdoms, such as animal and mineral.</note>, just as when a horse and donkey mate, this sterile animal is born. Nothing but a mineral may come to the **Mineral Stone** <note>The alchemical "stone" or tincture derived purely from metallic/mineral sources, intended for the transmutation of metals.</note>, which has everything within itself to make itself perfect. Whoever does otherwise does violence to nature and will find himself deceived in the end, for he will not obtain what he has desired and hoped for.

### 189. What other kinds of glass are made from it?

**Answer:** However one desires them, according to the common manner: with **potash** <note>Potassium carbonate, a vital ingredient in traditional glassmaking.</note>, a blue one is made with **silver** <note>original: ☽ (the lunar symbol for silver)</note>. But a much more beautiful one, produced through **calcination** <note>The process of heating a substance to high temperatures to reduce it to a powder or ash.</note> and **extraction**, is a glass of rainbow colors, which shines like burning fiery mirrors in the sun and by light.

### 191. If then there is only one matter that contains within itself the gold-glue and red gum, does it not have special signs by which one can recognize it?

**Answer:** Even if there is only one **matter**, one must still know the **diagnostic signs** <note>original: *Signa diagnostica*</note> in **selecting the matter** <note>original: *eligenda Materia*</note>; otherwise, the beginning is a failure. These shine in our **electrum** <note>original: *Electro*; in alchemy, this refers to a specialized metallic composition or alloy believed to possess celestial or magical properties.</note> like the stars in this solar heaven with their twinkling. ...and no one will know it better than I, because I myself have traveled for it and crawled through many mountains where it is quarried, especially in the **Saxon lands** <note>Saxony was a historical center for mining and alchemical research in Germany</note>. I wish to give here an infallible sign: when it is dissolved in **aqua fortis** <note>original: AF.; literally "strong water," the alchemical name for nitric acid</note>, and after the **dissolution** <note>original: Solution</note>, when the **sediment** has settled, the **aqua fortis** looks a beautiful rose color; then it is the right **matter** <note>original: Materia</note>. It is not all equally rich; if it is of the best kind, one can have 12 to 16 **loth** <note>a traditional German unit of weight, approximately 1/32 of a pound or 14.6 grams</note> from a pound that serves the work. But if it is poor in beautiful colors and only shimmers whitish, it has less—perhaps only 8 or 6 loth of the beautiful colors in it—of which much more could be said in confidence.

### CXCII. ->If one may not take the whole **body** <note>original: Corpus; the bulk physical matter</note>, how does one do it then?<-

**Answer:** One must know a **solvent** <note>original: Menstruum; a liquid used to dissolve or extract specific components from a solid</note> in which to purify it, so that it does not take up the rest, but only its **blood and life** <note>alchemical terms for the essential "soul" or tincture of the metal</note>.

### CXCIII. ->Is it then to be treated in only one way?<-

**Answer:** There is not just one way for the first purification, and here there are many delays where some toil for many weeks; yet it can also be performed in 8 days and even shorter. In the subsequent work, however, there is only one way in the **composition** <note>the process of combining the purified elements</note>.

### CXCIV. ->But you have written of the **wet and dry paths**?<-

**Answer:** It is certainly wet at first, but afterward...

...dry, there is in truth <note>original: re vera</note> only one way. How does one intend to purify the **Matter** <note>original: Materiam; the raw alchemical substance</note> in dry form? How can it strike the **dregs** <note>original: Feces; the impurities or waste matter</note> to the bottom? And even if one could do it dry in a great fire, its **coloring soul** <note>original: Anima tingens; the essential spirit or "tincture" that allows the substance to transform metals</note> would nevertheless be lost, and one would keep only the empty nest, which is exactly what must refine and color its **Mercury** <term>☿ Mercurium: not common quicksilver, but the "philosophical" mercury representing the fluid principle of metals</term> so that it can color in turn. One must also spread it out and make it thin, so that it becomes the "heaven" through which this "sun" can pass, making it into glass, into a glassy sea; for our metallic gold is not made with pebbles, but with our glass-like **Azoth** <note>a term for the "universal solvent" or the essential spirit of mercury</note>, which has an entry into the metals. It is our Water and Salt-Stone of Nature, which is so metallic that it penetrates into the innermost parts of the metals.

Only a few have written of the "dry path"; they were moved to do so when they melted it with **Iron** <term>♂ Marte; literally "Mars," the alchemical symbol for iron</term> and **Antimony** <term>🜔 Antimonio; the mineral stibnite</term>, brought the **Antimony** <term>♁ Antimonium; here referring to the purified metal or "regulus"</term> out of the slag, and melted it anew with the metallic starters until they **vitrified** <note>original: vetrificirt; turned into a glass-like substance</note> them together. Having applied it **part for part** <note>original: pars cum parte; in equal proportions</note> and found something with the consistency of wax on **Gold** <term>☉ Sole</term> and **Silver** <term>☽ Luna</term>, they immediately imagined the Stone could be made in this way. 

But this will not work at all; it requires other labor and understanding. If it were so simple and plain, all the **amateur furnace-tenders** <note>original: Uncken-Brenner; a derogatory term for clumsy or fraudulent alchemists, literally "toad-burners"</note> would have had it long ago. ### CXCV. How then does one dissolve this solvent? <note>original: solvirt man denn dieses Menstruum; a "menstruum" is an alchemical term for a solvent capable of dissolving solids</note>

**Antw.** The pearls are crushed and dissolved in **Virgin’s Milk** <term>Jungfer-Milch: a poetic name for various white or cloudy alchemical liquids, often an acidic solution or a lead-based water</term>; thereupon one can pour a **spirit of wine** <note>original: Spiritum vini; concentrated alcohol or brandy</note> and digest it, then distill it over, so that a pearl-milk comes over, and finally a yellow oil. Both of these are used for a stroke or for lost speech to bring such things back; a **cordial** <note>a medicine intended to invigorate the heart and spirit</note> the likes of which is not to be found, except for the **Universal Medicine** <note>original: Universal; the fabled "Panacea" or Philosopher's Stone capable of curing all ills</note>.

### CXCVI. How then does one dissolve the metals therein?

**Antw.** This dissolution <note>original: Zerschmelzung; literally "melting down," but used here for chemical liquefaction</note> does not happen by force, but gradually; they melt away within it and become like oil and salves—the **Gold** <term>☉ Sol; the alchemical symbol for the sun and gold</term> in red water, and the **Silver** <term>☽ Luna; the alchemical symbol for the moon and silver</term> in white water. The metals may also first have been reduced to **calx** <term>Kalck: a powder produced by heating a metal or mineral in air; a metallic oxide</term>.
