In the Piedmont mountains, it is called *Magnesia pia montana* <note>Latin: Magnesia of the pious mountains. In this period, "Magnesia" usually referred to manganese dioxide, used by glassmakers to create purple tints or to clear impurities</note>. To some it appears gray-black. Glass made from it takes on purple and amethyst colors; when melted and boiled out with <term>Antimony</term><note>Symbol: 🜔</note>, it yields a purple color. The solution changes colors: it is grass-green, then becomes sky-blue, violet-colored, and rose-red. In Friuli, there is a Magnesia of <term>Lead</term><note>Symbol: ♄</note> that is much purer and better than that in Piedmont; it is grayish. <term>Antimony</term><note>Symbol: 🜔</note> draws out a beautiful green color from it, which transforms into blue, red, and then back into green. It is also found between Swabia and Bavaria, where it is thoroughly grown through with a volatile lead ore. It is also found in Bohemia, Saxony, Saalfeld, Eichsfeld, Hungary, and other places; in the Kinzig Valley near Strasbourg—where a color mill <note>a mill used for grinding minerals into pigments</note> has also been built—and many other locations besides. One must only choose the right kind, as it has many names. However, no one here should imagine, simply because I mention color mills, that I mean the poisonous black cobalt <note>original: Cobold; often regarded by early miners as a deceptive or "goblin" mineral that mimicked silver but was toxic</note>. I only provide guidance to inquire into the matter because such Magnesia often breaks out in cobalt veins. It also has its own distinct vein; however, where cobalt is mixed with kupfernickel <note>original: Kupffer eickel; a nickel arsenide ore</note>, our "Matter" is not to be found there, for it is adulterated with other "dark spirits," just as are the types of bismuth which are stained by other substances.

**CXCVIII.** <note>198</note> How is it then to be understood when they say, this Stone is everything, and is also nothing?

**Answer.** It has this meaning: It is everything if one knows its preparation <note>original: Bereitung</note>, and it is also nothing if one does not understand its labor—even if one understands the authors well and knows the entire work perfectly in one's head. However, when one undertakes the practice <note>original: Praxin</note>, secret knots appear to be untied, and things are seen that enchant the sight, as it were, so that one does not know the way out. There are certain terms, times, and hours determined by nature which one may not exceed, all of which I have indeed indicated in my previous little treatises <note>original: Tractätlein</note> and also here, and have revealed everything as clear as day. Yet it will still be difficult if someone wishes to rush in with an "unwashed mouth" <note>an idiom meaning to speak or act without proper preparation, reverence, or authority</note> and kiss the naked **Diana** <note>In alchemy, Diana (the goddess of the moon) represents the pure, white stage of the matter or the secret heart of Nature; to see her "naked" is to uncover the ultimate secret of the Work</note>. It is no labor, as has been said, for peasants who only know how to grease a wagon; one must be well-experienced in the **fire**, have burned one's fingers, and have read many a fine book, otherwise he will lack even the basic stride of a good peasant.

**CXCIX.** <note>199</note> But how can a single medicine <note>original: Arzene</note> help against all illnesses <note>original: Kranckheiten</note>, and all people without distinction?

**Answer.** Since bread helps all people against hunger, and water helps all people against thirst, so too can this **Universal Medicine** <note>original: Universal-Medic; the text cuts off here and continues on the next page</note>

...fallen away; <note>This completes a thought from the previous page regarding the falling away of symptoms or diseases.</note> our remedy does not do any of these things—whether it be white or red <note>referring to the "White" or "Red" Elixirs of alchemy, used to create silver or gold and heal the body</note>—it works according to the <term>inclination of nature <note>original: Inclination der Natur; the body's natural constitution or inherent tendencies</note></term>, without vomiting or <term>purging <note>original: Purgiren; the medical practice of using laxatives to "clear" the body</note></term>. It induces sweat, does not cause exhaustion, strengthens nature, and assists it in an imperceptible way; it renovates, restores, drives away old age, introduces youth, maintains one's strength, and through the beam of its light, drives out all evil.

**CC.** Are all the secret keys of nature discovered herein, so that through them one can explore the secrets of the entire natural world within the <term>mineral kingdom <note>original: mineralischen Reiche</note></term>?

**Ans.** Yes, as much as has been revealed to me concerning the mineral kingdom by God the Most High, and which I have received through God’s grace, I have handed these over to my neighbor. Yet, I commit such keys to the Divine Majesty, before whose high throne I lay them down in deepest humility, leaving it to His Divine Omnipotence to decide to whom He wishes to hand them further and bestow them as a gift. I thank Him with heart and mouth for His great love and grace, with which He has gifted me, a poor, unworthy <term>earthworm <note>original: Erdwurm; a common historical expression of extreme religious humility</note></term>. But above all, I thank Him that He brought me to the <term>knowledge <note>original: Erkänntniß</note></term> of Him, so that in His light, I have beheld Him as the true Light. And I fervently pray to the most holy God to keep me constantly in love and faith in Him, so that when my spirit, soul, and body are separated <note>The text breaks here at "schie-", completing the word "geschieden" or "separated" on the following page.</note> from one another in death—

<summary>The author describes the gentle, restorative power of the alchemical medicine and concludes the discourse by attributing his knowledge of the "keys of nature" to divine grace.</summary>

## Appendix.

The <term>Philosophers <note>original: Philosophi; here referring to alchemists</note></term> or <term>Adepts <note>original: Adepti; those who have mastered the secrets of alchemy</note></term> write that a Lead Mineral <note>original: Bley Minera</note> or metallic essence is to be found in nature which is easily dissolved; whoever understands its <term>solution <note>original: Solution; the process of dissolving a solid into liquid form</note></term> and how to moisten it with the water of its own kind would be a blessed Physician <note>original: Medicus</note>. This subject they call the **Lead of the Philosophers** <note>original: Plumbum Philosophorum</note>, also designated by the symbol ♁ <note>The symbol for the planet Earth or the mineral Antimony, often identified as the "First Matter" in this tradition</note>, because its "female," or the "Lily" <note>original: Lili; a term often used for the purified white stage of a substance</note> of the ancients, is the <term>marcasite <note>original: Marcasitta; in historical alchemy, this often referred to bismuth or various sulfide ores like pyrite or stibnite</note></term> *par excellence* <note>original: per excellentiam; by way of excellence</note>. Now this mineral is found in its "little gray smocks" <note>a metaphorical description of the dull, rocky outer crust of the ore</note> with the iridescent colors of a pigeon's neck; inside it has beautiful yellow veins and most glorious little sparks, which shine and shimmer like stars in this solar heaven. It is also found in nature—when it is old and has stood past its time—to be consumed by the <term>natural acid <note>original: Acido der Natur</note></term>, which the miners call **Cobalt-Bloom** <note>original: Cobold-Blüthe; likely erythrite, a vivid purple-red mineral</note>, and also **Rose-Bloom** <note>original: Rosen-Blüthe</note>. I have also seen such stones which were thinly [coated] with this material... <note>The text cuts off here at the word "ein-", indicating a word like "wrapped" or "encrusted" continues on the next page.</note>

<summary>This page concludes a prayer for spiritual enlightenment before beginning an appendix that describes the "Lead of the Philosophers," an essential mineral substance in alchemy identified by its iridescent colors and specific mineral formations known as "Cobalt-Bloom."</summary>

# New Books,
### Which are available from this same publisher.

The Learned and Gallant State Society, which impartially communicates its reflections on foreign and domestic, theological and political, old and new, commendable and blameworthy, wise and foolish matters to the <term>curious world <note>original: curieusen Welt; the 18th-century "curious" public referred to educated readers interested in news, science, and secrets of nature</note></term> in various discourses; it also passes wise yet thorough judgments on old and new books. Octavo.

->Of this, 8 issues have been released, and it is continued monthly.<-

<term>Legal Pronouncements and Advices <note>original: Enunciata & Consilia Juris</note></term> of various legal scholars, famous faculties, and <term>courts of lay-judges <note>original: Schöppen-Stühle; traditional municipal or regional courts in German-speaking lands</note></term>, regarding special and <term>dubious cases <note>original: dubiœse casus</note></term> occurring in general life; collected and explained with notes by <term>Putoneo <note>a pseudonym used by the author, common in the Enlightenment to avoid censorship or maintain professional modesty</note></term>. 6th part. Octavo.
